# CITAR 0.1.6, Phase 5: packaging, CI, deploy, docs (design)

**Status.** Design, 2026-10-18, for Phase 5 of `ops/plan-0.1.6.md` §7, written against `v0.1.6` at `51ce329` (Phases 0-4 complete; Phase 4's exit met in full, its deferred real-model gate passed at `8061578`); revised the same day after its review (P5.23 maps each finding to its fix and package). Sections are numbered P5.x, so they cannot be confused with DESIGN.md's Phase 1 (§1-§12), Phase 2 (P2.x), `design/phase3-features.md` (P3.x) or `design/phase4-helper.md` (P4.x). Appendix F is the work breakdown: fifteen packages, run strictly one after another, one agent at a time on this laptop (the owner's standing choice). Each package appends an "As built in 5-xx" note to the section it implements, records its wall-clock start and end, and ends with its gates, a review and a fix round.

This file lives in `design/`, outside `docs/`, so mkdocs and the wiki generator never see it. It names code paths in backticks rather than linking them, so `scripts/check_links.py` needs no change. It names no private detail of the VPS (address, accounts, key paths, the other sites' names): those stay in the gitignored `ops/DEPLOY_LOG.md`, and every script here takes them as arguments or from a file on the box.

**Sources:**
- `ops/plan-0.1.6.md` §1 (R4: the tap ships only the helper; the game installs from prebuilt wheels through `install.sh` or pipx), §7 Phase 5 (builds, VPS, docs, exit) and Phase 6 (what this phase must make mechanical), §9 risk 5 (the VPS runs wheels only).
- `crates/citar-engine/DESIGN.md` P2.6.7 and P2.6.8 (maturin, the CI's builds), "As built in 2-06b" (check_dist, the manylinux_2_28 wheel, the sdist's contents, "Left for later"), P2.9 "Packaging: now and Phase 5", P2.2.1 (the content build id; `CITAR_BUILD_ID` is a display label).
- `design/phase4-helper.md` P4.3.8 (the manifest, the `packaged` feature, `keygen`/`helper-manifest`/`sign-manifest`), P4.3.9 (services), P4.7 (the cross-cutting rules this design keeps), P4.10 "What Phase 5 builds", and the as-built notes of 4-13 and 4-14 (the CI signing path, the five release names, helper.yml).
- `design/phase3-features.md`'s Phase 5-6 notes (the release docs pass), `KNOWN_ISSUES.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`'s Unreleased section, `docs/server/WORKERS.md` "Signing helper updates".
- `ops/phase1-common.md`, `ops/p3-notes.md` and the last sections of `ops/p4-notes.md` (the build environment and the CI lessons: CI slack x3, `perf_counter`, `TZ=UTC0` and `C:/dev/venv/py312-check`, `fsutil.newest_first`, never sleep-then-assert, elevated Windows runners, macOS soft limits, `cargo doc -D warnings` on Linux, `cargo build` of a bin with required features needing `--bin` and `--features`), and `ops/DEPLOY_LOG.md` (the VPS as deployed, read for facts only).
- The current tree, read file by file (P5.0 cites it).

---

## P5.0 What exists today (checked against the tree at `51ce329`)

### P5.0.1 Versions and the build label

- **Two version sources held equal:** `[workspace.package] version = "0.1.5"` in `Cargo.toml` (every crate takes `version.workspace = true`) and `__version__ = "0.1.5"` in `citar/__init__.py`; `cargo xtask check` (`xtask/src/check/version.rs`) fails when they differ. `pyproject.toml` has `dynamic = ["version"]`, so the wheel's version is Cargo's.
- **Other places that carry the version, held by nothing:** the eleven workspace members' entries in `Cargo.lock`, and the three path packages (`citar-bot`, `citar-engine`, `citar-testkit`) in the fuzz crate's own `crates/citar-engine/fuzz/Cargo.lock`, which rust.yml checks `--locked`; `installer/citar.iss` (`#define AppVersion "0.1.5"` at `:15`, the comment `ISCC.exe /DAppVersion=0.1.5` at `:4`, and `VersionInfoVersion={#AppVersion}` at `:34`, which Inno Setup requires to be numeric); `docs/INSTALL.md:33` (`Get-FileHash .\CITAR-0.1.5-setup.exe`); the `KNOWN_ISSUES.md` header ("in 0.1.6"); the CHANGELOG's section and compare links. The 0.1.5 procedure bumped them by hand.
- **The engine's content code hashes the package version** (DESIGN.md P2.2.1), so a version bump moves the build id (`0b412a18fe95` today) and the jobs build. Nothing pins them where a bump would break it: the `jobs` golden set holds a result's build-naming keys to the running build's values (`crates/citar-jobs/tests/golden.rs:9-11`, "an edit that moves the build id and changes no game needs no bless"); `citar/jobs/costs.json`'s `build` is informational (`citar/jobs/store.py` never reads it); no Python test pins `__version__`.
- **The label:** `BUILD_LABEL` is `CITAR_BUILD_ID` at compile time, else `unknown` (`crates/citar-engine/src/rules/source.rs:71-74`). `cargo xtask develop` sets it from `git describe` (`xtask/src/develop.rs:386`); no CI build sets it, so every CI wheel and helper says `unknown` (2-06b's "Left for later": "the tag's label is Phase 5's"). It is shown by `citar doctor` (`citar/doctor.py:241`: "build X (label), version V"), carried in `build_info()`, and printed by `citar-helper --version` and its hello (`crates/citar-helper/src/version.rs:79-121`).

### P5.0.2 The package and its builds in CI

- **`pyproject.toml`** builds with maturin (`maturin>=1.15,<2`): the mixed layout, `module-name = "citar._engine"`, `features = []`, `editable-profile = "ci"`, the `include` lists, `license-files = ["LICENSE", "NOTICE.md"]`, `requires-python = ">=3.11"`, classifiers through 3.14. The extension is `abi3-py311` (`Cargo.toml`'s `pyo3` line): one wheel per platform serves 3.11 and every later CPython (not the free-threaded builds, which abi3 does not cover).
- **`test.yml`** (`MATURIN_VERSION: v1.15.0`):
  - `build-ext` (`:41-125`) builds the ci-profile test wheel with `test-ops` on ubuntu, windows and macos, and the dev-guard helpers for the end-to-end tests;
  - `test` (`:127-207`) runs the suite on Linux 3.11, 3.12 and 3.13 and on Windows and macOS 3.12; `interpreter-exit` covers 3.11-3.14; `lint` is ruff; `frontend` is Node;
  - `package` (`:290-395`) builds the sdist and **one** release wheel, manylinux_2_28 x86_64, and checks both with `scripts/ci/check_dist.py`;
  - `installer` (`:397-440`) runs `install.sh --from` with the **build-ext test wheel** (ci profile) on ubuntu and macOS;
  - `docker` (`:442-482`) builds the image from the manylinux wheel for amd64 only and checks its health route.
- **Not built anywhere:** the Windows, macOS and Linux aarch64 release wheels. No wheel is smoke-tested on the Python floor (3.11) or the newest (3.14), and no suite runs on 3.14, which the classifiers claim.

### P5.0.3 release.yml

The workflow (`.github/workflows/release.yml`, 398 lines) is 0.1.5's with Phase 4's helper jobs, behind a gate:
- **`verify`'s first step fails every run** while `__version__` is 0.1.6 or earlier ("release packaging is Phase 5", `:36-57`), and every job needs `verify`. Its suite runs on `pip install -e ".[all]"`, an editable ci-profile build **without** the test operations, so the engine's tests skip there.
- **`build-python`** (`:88-101`) runs `python -m build` on Linux: one `linux_x86_64` wheel (no manylinux tag; PyPI refuses it) and the sdist.
- **`build-windows`** (`:116-180`) bundles an **editable ci-profile** extension built into the checkout (`pip install -e`), so a bundle would ship the ci profile; Inno Setup's path is hard-coded to `Inno Setup 6`.
- **`build-helper`** (`:193-262`) is P4.10's five-target cargo matrix (4-14). No `CITAR_BUILD_ID`, so the label is `unknown`. The Linux builds run on `ubuntu-22.04` and `ubuntu-22.04-arm` (helper.yml's `artifact` matrix too), so they need glibc 2.35, while `install.sh` accepts 2.28 for the game.
- **`sign-helper-manifest`** (`:272-314`) names `environment: helper-signing` **with no tag guard**: once the gate goes, a dry run would create that environment (it does not exist; a job naming a missing environment creates it) and, once protected, wait for the owner's approval. Its signing step exports `CITAR_UPDATE_SIGNING_KEY` and runs `cargo xtask sign-manifest` in the same step; `cargo xtask` is `cargo run --package xtask --` (`.cargo/config.toml`), so every build script and proc macro in xtask's dependency tree compiles with the release key in its environment.
- **Every action is referenced by a moving tag** (`actions/checkout@v7`, `docker/login-action@v4`, `softprops/action-gh-release@v3`, `PyO3/maturin-action@v1`, ...), and `pypa/gh-action-pypi-publish@release/v1` by a branch, in the jobs that hold PyPI's OIDC token or write permissions as in every other workflow. Dependabot's `github-actions` updates already run weekly (`.github/dependabot.yml`), and update SHA pins as readily as tags.
- **`publish-image`** (`:317-354`) builds from the source under QEMU for arm64; the Dockerfile has since required `wheelhouse/` (2-06b), so it would fail.
- **`github-release`** (`:357-398`) collects `*.whl`, `*.tar.gz`, `*.exe`, `*.zip`, `*.sha256` and `citar-helper-*`; the notes are the CHANGELOG section (awk) plus checksums. `publish-pypi`, `publish-image` and `github-release` are guarded by `startsWith(github.ref, 'refs/tags/')` only: a `workflow_dispatch` **run on a tag ref** would pass that guard and publish.
- No attestations, no `SHA256SUMS`, no check that the build ids of the artifacts agree.

### P5.0.4 The Windows bundle and installer

- **`installer/citar.spec`** puts the checkout first on `sys.path` (`:28`) and in both `pathex` (`:84`, `:96`), so PyInstaller collects `citar` **from the checkout**, which holds `_engine.pyd` only after an editable build. Nothing names `citar._engine` or checks it was collected.
- **`installer/citar.iss`**: per-user (`PrivilegesRequired=lowest`), the PyInstaller folder whole, an unchecked "add to PATH" task (HKCU `Environment`), the uninstaller asking before deleting `%LOCALAPPDATA%\CITAR` (default No).
- **The bundle's lab cannot run** (P5.0.11).

### P5.0.5 The image

`Dockerfile` installs the dependencies from `pyproject.toml` (`scripts/ci/requirements.py server`), then `citar` from `wheelhouse/` with `--no-index` (`:50-54`), on `python:3.12-slim-bookworm` (Debian 12, past its regular security support since mid-2026); runs as uid 10001, health on `/api/auth/config`. `docker-compose.yml` pulls `ghcr.io/jprodgers/citar:latest` behind Caddy (`deploy/Caddyfile`). Only amd64 is ever built from a wheel.

### P5.0.6 The install scripts

- **`install.sh`** makes a venv under `~/.local/share/citar-app` (or `/opt/citar` with `--server`), installs `citar[all]` (or `[server]`) **from PyPI with no `--only-binary`** (`:176-179`), so on a platform without a wheel pip downloads the sdist and fails slowly at the Rust build; links the commands into `~/.local/bin` and appends a PATH line to the shell profile (`:200-232`); `--from` takes a local wheel or checkout (CI's route); `--worker` (`:59-65`) only prints where to download the helper and exits 2.
- **`install.ps1`** installs into `%LOCALAPPDATA%\CITAR\app` and writes the **user's PATH** (`:38-39`, `:149`): it has no `-Prefix`, no `-From` and no way to leave the PATH alone, so it cannot be tested on this laptop at all without touching the owner's real profile; `-Worker` prints the pointer.

### P5.0.7 Package managers and `release_checksums.py`

- `packaging/homebrew/citar.rb` installs **the whole game from the PyPI sdist** into a virtualenv (`:14`): since 0.1.6 that sdist needs a Rust toolchain the formula does not depend on, and R4 says the tap ships only the helper.
- `packaging/scoop/citar.json` (the portable zip) and `packaging/winget/` (three files, the Inno installer, `JimmieRodgers.CITAR`) carry the game only, at 0.1.5. Microsoft never merged a New-package PR for `JimmieRodgers.CITAR` (0.1.0 to 0.1.5 open).
- `scripts/release_checksums.py` hashes three assets (the PyPI sdist, the setup.exe, the zip: `ASSETS`, `:27-41`) and rewrites the three manifests in place; the sdist URL can 404 for a while after a release.

### P5.0.8 The helper's release side (P4.10's hooks)

- The five unversioned names (`citar-helper-windows-x64.exe`, `-macos-arm64`, `-macos-x64`, `-linux-x64`, `-linux-arm64`) are held equal by `citar/web/tests/helper.test.mjs` across `helperrules.js`, release.yml's `build-helper` matrix, helper.yml's `artifact` matrix and `cargo xtask helper-manifest`'s table.
- **The `packaged` feature** exists (`crates/citar-helper/Cargo.toml`; `config.rs:536`, `update/mod.rs:567`): `updates` defaults to `notify` and `auto` is refused. **Nothing builds it.**
- **xtask:** `keygen --out DIR` (refuses a folder in any git working tree), `helper-manifest --assets DIR --version V` (reads the build, protocol, version and target from the Linux asset, which must run where it is called), `sign-manifest (--key FILE | --key-env NAME)`. There is no verify command; the extension's `verify_manifest` trusts only the compiled-in keys (and, with test-ops, `CITAR_HELPER_UPDATE_KEY`).
- **`crates/citar-jobs/update-keys.pub` is empty**, so every helper reports updates `unavailable`. The owner's setup (environment `helper-signing` with a required reviewer and the secret `CITAR_UPDATE_SIGNING_KEY`, the public key committed) is `docs/server/WORKERS.md`'s "Signing helper updates"; the environment does not exist yet.
- `cargo xtask check` (`xtask/src/check/helper.rs`) refuses a test feature, `--all-features` or a feature list held in a variable on any line of release.yml and of helper.yml's artifact jobs; `check/triggers.rs` holds rust.yml's path filters to every file the checks read.

### P5.0.9 The deploy and the VPS

- **On the box** (facts from `ops/DEPLOY_LOG.md`; private details stay there): Ubuntu 24.04.5, 1 vCPU, 961 MB RAM, a 2 GB swapfile, x86_64, Python 3.12.3; nginx with certbot's TLS; three other sites that must never be disturbed. CITAR's code is a `git archive` of the tag extracted into `/opt/citar` with a venv at `/opt/citar/.venv`; state in `/var/lib/citar`; configuration and secrets in `/etc/citar/citar.env` (root:citar 0640); `citar.service` (user `citar`, `MemoryMax=400M`, a sandbox); a permanent `citar-lab.service` (0.1.5's runner, `MemoryMax=450M` of its own); `citar-backup` service and timer; a hand-made `/usr/local/bin/citar-admin` wrapper.
- **The tracked files** (`deploy/`): `citar.service` runs `python -m citar.server` with `WorkingDirectory=/opt/citar` (`:14-16`), so a wheel install would be shadowed by any `citar/` left in that tree (P2.9); `citar-lab.service` is already gone (4-09); `citar-backup.sh` snapshots the database, the env file and `saves/` only, so `config/`, `benchmarks/` and anything else 0.1.6 writes under the state directory is not backed up; `citar-secrets.sh`; `nginx-citar.conf`; `Caddyfile` (compose only).
- **The wizard's unit** (`citar/wizard/server.py:348-382`, `citar setup --server`) is a second, different definition (`Type=simple`, no `WorkingDirectory`, a shorter sandbox).
- **`requirements.txt`** exists only because "the service runs the code in place from /opt/citar" (its header); `scripts/sync_requirements.py` and test.yml's step keep it equal to `pyproject.toml`.
- **The procedure** (`docs/server/RUNBOOK.md:235-252`, the private log): `git archive`, `scp`, backup, extract, `pip install -r requirements.txt`, restart `citar` and `citar-lab`, check the other sites answer 200.
- **What 0.1.6 needs instead of `citar-lab`:** nothing. The lab runs inside the server: the dispatcher's local executor starts one job host child (`python -m citar.jobs host`, `citar/jobs/local.py`, `citar/jobs/host.py`) inside `citar.service`'s cgroup, under the host machine's CPU settings, server mode's default being one thread, 50% and min(1,024 MB, a quarter of the RAM), so about 240 MB on this box, held by `RLIMIT_DATA` (a system service has no user manager for a scope) and `nice` (`citar/machine_settings.py:159-186`). So `MemoryMax=400M` now holds the server **and** its games, where 0.1.5 gave CITAR 400M + 450M.
- **What 0.1.6 lost with `citar-lab`:** 0.1.5's lab unit (`git show v0.1.5:deploy/citar-lab.service`) protected the other sites with `Nice=10`, `CPUWeight=20`, `IOWeight=20`, `MemoryMax=450M`, `MemoryHigh=380M` and `OOMScoreAdjust=800`. `citar.service` sets no weights (the default 100), and its `MemoryHigh=320M` throttles the whole cgroup, the web server included, before `MemoryMax=400M`. The job host's 50% is a thread cap and `nice`, not a hard limit: once any unit's CPU setting has enabled the cpu controller in `system.slice`, `nice` ranks the host only against the server's own threads, not against the other sites' services. The private log's 0.1.5 measurement (the lab at nice 10 taking about 95% of the CPU, the other sites still answering in 0.3-0.4 s, about 240 MB available) is the bar 0.1.6 must meet.
- **The server migrates on every start** (`citar/server/boot.py:55-110`, `command.upgrade(cfg, "head")`) and refuses a database at a revision its scripts do not know (a newer build's): once a release has migrated the database, an older release cannot start on it.
- **The job host is started with `-m`** (`citar/jobs/host.py:86`), which puts the working directory first on `sys.path`; under a unit whose working directory is the state directory, that is a service-writable folder ahead of site-packages.

### P5.0.10 Docs and the wiki

`README.md`, `docs/INSTALL.md` and `packaging/README.md` describe 0.1.5's routes (Homebrew installing the game, `pip install` from an sdist that compiled nothing, no helper packages); `docs/server/DEPLOY.md` and `RUNBOOK.md` the `git archive` deploy; `CONTRIBUTING.md`'s "Releasing" (`:554-560`) the hand bump. `NOTICE.md` says "No dependency is vendored", while every wheel and helper now statically links about 150 Rust crates whose licences (MIT, Apache-2.0, BSD, ISC, Unicode) require their notices to travel with the binaries, and the Windows bundle carries the Python dependencies' code. `KNOWN_ISSUES.md` "Platforms and packaging" predates the wheels. The wiki is generated by `scripts/build_wiki.py` from 25 pages; mkdocs builds the site from `docs/`.

### P5.0.11 Defects found while reading (fixed in Phase 5)

1. **The Windows bundle's lab cannot start its job host.** `citar/jobs/host.py:86` starts `[sys.executable, "-m", "citar.jobs", "host"]`; in a PyInstaller bundle `sys.executable` is `citar.exe` or `citar-play.exe`, whose argument parser refuses `-m`. Every lab game of a bundled install fails at the host's start (5-05).
2. **`/api/meta` names a file no installed copy has and leaks paths.** It returns `mcp_script = <root>/citar_mcp.py` with `root = Path(__file__).parents[2]` (`citar/server/app.py:481-487`): in a wheel that is `site-packages/citar_mcp.py`, which does not exist (the file is the checkout's), so the lobby's MCP command (`citar/web/js/lobby.js:221-228`) is wrong for every pip, pipx, bundle and image install; and the route is public (`scripts/audit_routes.py:47`), so a public server tells anyone its install path and interpreter (5-05).
3. **A `workflow_dispatch` on a tag would publish** (P5.0.3); **a dry run would create `helper-signing`** (P5.0.3).
4. **Release builds carry the label `unknown`** (P5.0.1).
5. **No third-party notices ship with the binaries** (P5.0.10).
6. **The backup misses part of the state directory** (P5.0.9).
7. **`install.ps1` cannot avoid the user's PATH and profile** (P5.0.6), so nothing tests it outside CI.
8. **The Homebrew formula cannot build 0.1.6** (P5.0.7).
9. **Two unit definitions** (P5.0.9) that already differ.
10. **The signing key reaches every xtask build script** (P5.0.3).
11. **Every action is a moving reference** (P5.0.3).
12. **The Linux helpers need glibc 2.35** where the game needs 2.28 (P5.0.3), so `--worker` on RHEL 8 and 9, Rocky, Alma or Debian 11 installs a helper that cannot start.
13. **The lab no longer yields the CPU to the other sites** and `MemoryHigh` throttles the server before its games reach `MemoryMax` (P5.0.9).
14. **The job host's `-m` puts the working directory on the import path** (P5.0.9).

### P5.0.12 This laptop, WSL and GitHub

- **Windows 11 x64:** Python 3.11, 3.12 and 3.14 (py launcher; PyInstaller in the global 3.14), Rust per `rust-toolchain.toml`, Node 24, gh, maturin 1.15. **No Docker, no Podman, no Inno Setup.** Nothing is installed on Windows; a package's venv (`C:/dev/venv/p-5-xx`) takes pip packages as every package's did.
- **WSL Ubuntu 24.04.5** (`wsl -d Ubuntu-24.04 -u claude`), Python 3.12.3 and systemd 255 as on the VPS, NAT networking, `claude` with `Linger=no`, 12 CPUs and 7.8 GB, `vm.swappiness` 60 (the VPS's is 10); the root cgroup offers `cpu memory pids` (no `io`, no `cpuset`) and `system.slice` delegates `memory pids` until a unit asks for a CPU setting. So `host_cpu_defaults()` there would size the job host for 12 CPUs and 7.8 GB, `IOWeight=` and `AllowedCPUs=` cannot be proven in WSL at all, and nothing binds until it is checked. **Missing, read at `51ce329`:** podman, docker, nginx, sqlite3, shellcheck, ruby, and `python3-venv` (as on a fresh VPS). Root (`wsl -u root`, inside WSL only) may `apt install` what a gate needs; each install is recorded in `ops/p5-notes.md`. Eight Phase 4 target folders sit in `~/target`; WSL's virtual disk grows and never shrinks by itself.
- **GitHub:** `jprodgers/CITAR` is public; environments `github-pages` (docs.yml's deploy from `main`) and `pypi` (PyPI's trusted publishing works through release.yml and `pypi`); **no `helper-signing`**; PR #5 (`v0.1.6` → `main`) runs test.yml on every push and rust.yml, helper.yml and determinism.yml on pushes their path filters match; releases v0.1.0 to v0.1.5 exist (0.1.5's with 14 assets); the image is `ghcr.io/jprodgers/citar`.

## P5.1 Goals, non-goals and exit

### P5.1.1 What Phase 5 delivers

1. **One release build** (`package.yml`, P5.3-P5.10) that makes every artifact a release ships, tests each on its own platform, and publishes nothing: five abi3 wheels built natively and the sdist, the Windows bundle and installer, ten helper builds (the five self-updating binaries and five `packaged` archives for package managers), the helper manifest with a dry-run signature, the image for amd64 and arm64, the install scripts run against those files, the package managers' manifests generated and installed from them, third-party notices, checksums and the release notes. It runs on every pull request, so no failure is left for the release to find.
2. **`release.yml`** (P5.11) reduced to a version check, the test and package workflows, and tag-only publishing jobs: the real signature, PyPI, the image, attestations, the GitHub release. A dry run (`workflow_dispatch`) builds everything and publishes nothing, creates no environment and waits for nobody.
3. **One version, one label** (P5.2): `scripts/release.py` checks and bumps every place the version lives; the label is `v<version>` on a tag.
4. **The deploy from the release wheel** (P5.13): a release layout on the server, units that cannot be shadowed by a source tree, scripted install, cutover from 0.1.5, rollback and checks, and a staging dry run of all of it in WSL under the VPS's limits (P5.14).
5. **The release checklist** Phase 6 follows (P5.15), every step a script or a command, each proven by a dry run.
6. **The docs pass** (P5.16), the wiki regenerated.

### P5.1.2 Owner decisions that bind this phase

- **R4:** the Homebrew tap ships only the prebuilt helper; the game installs from prebuilt wheels through `install.sh` or pipx.
- **Plan §7 Phase 5:** the maturin wheel matrix (win-x64, macOS arm64 + x64, manylinux x64 + aarch64 built natively); Docker from wheels; install scripts from wheels only; the Windows bundle carrying `_engine.pyd`; Scoop and winget; the helper matrix (the same names plus `macos-x64`); attestations; the VPS deploy installs the release wheel. **Exit:** a dry run of the release workflow green on every platform.
- **Risk 5:** the VPS runs wheels only; mitigation: CI-built wheels and a staging dry run in WSL.
- **The update-signing key** (owner's answer of 2026-10-14, 4-13): a GitHub Actions secret in the protected environment `helper-signing`, created by the owner, who commits the public key. Agents never make the real key, environments, secrets or repository settings.
- **Priorities:** stability and speed first; fix bugs rather than carry them; no other users yet, so packaging changes freely (no install compatibility with 0.1.5 is owed, but the CHANGELOG says what changes for someone coming from it); strictly one agent at a time; packages sized for one sitting with local, re-runnable gates; CI-only gates named.
- **Safety** (P5.18.1) and **dispatch** (P5.18.2) rules.

### P5.1.3 Non-goals (Phase 6's, or later)

- **Phase 6:** the soak and QA runs, the security review, the performance report against 0.1.5, **the VPS deploy itself** (with the owner's go-ahead), the version bump, the tag, PyPI, the image push, the GitHub release, the tap, the bucket and winget. Phase 5 makes each mechanical and proves it by a dry run; it performs none of them.
- **Not in 0.1.6:** code signing and notarisation (P5.6, KNOWN_ISSUES); musllinux, 32-bit, Windows ARM64 and free-threaded wheels (an unsupported platform is told so and pointed at the sdist); homebrew-core; a winget submission that Microsoft merges (not in our hands).

### P5.1.4 Exit criteria

| Criterion | Measured by |
|---|---|
| The release dry run is green on every platform | `gh workflow run release.yml --ref v0.1.6 -f version=0.1.6` (P5.18.2), watched to its end: `meta`, test.yml (Linux 3.11-3.14, Windows, macOS, interpreter-exit, lint, frontend) and every job of package.yml green, every tag-only job skipped, no environment named (every non-`pypi` environment is the tag expression, P5.11), the environments unchanged. The run id and each job's conclusion in "As built in 5-13". |
| Every wheel and the sdist | Five wheels, each tagged as P5.3's table says, checked by `check_dist.py`, installed in a clean venv on its own runner under Python 3.11 and 3.14 and smoke-tested (P5.3.4); the sdist checked and built into a wheel that passes the same smoke; the five wheels report one version, one label, one build id and one jobs build and play the same seeded lab job to the same result hash; the sdist's build reports the same version, build id, jobs build and result hash with the label `unknown`. |
| The bundle and the installer | The bundle built from the Windows wheel carries `_engine.pyd`, runs `--version`, `where` and `doctor` (its job host verifiable); the installer compiled, installed silently per user into a temporary folder on the runner, run, and uninstalled cleanly; the portable zip. |
| The helpers and the manifest | Ten builds on five runners (each `--version` naming its target, version and label, `--self-test`; the four Linux binaries built in the manylinux_2_28 containers and needing no glibc symbol newer than 2.28); the manifest over the five; its throwaway-key signature verified by xtask and by an independent verifier, a one-byte change refused. |
| The image | Built natively for amd64 and arm64 from the wheels, each run: health within 60 s, engine without test operations, the right version and label, the job host verifiable. |
| The install scripts | `install.sh` on Linux x64 and arm64 and macOS arm64 and x64, `install.ps1` on Windows, against the built wheels and helpers served from loopback; the game runs, `--worker` installs a helper that runs, uninstall leaves nothing. |
| The package managers | The Scoop, winget and Homebrew manifests generated from the built files and validated; Scoop installs the game and the helper on Windows, Homebrew installs and tests the helper on macOS arm64, macOS x64 and Linux x64; winget's manifests validated (and installed where the runner has winget). |
| The staging deploy | In WSL, as root, under a slice held to the VPS (P5.14: about 60% of one laptop core, 961 MB, 2 GB of swap at swappiness 10, the other sites' memory held by a ballast and their CPU by a loaded stand-in, every limit probed to bind): a 0.1.5 layout cut over to the **CI-built** wheel with `citar-deploy`, the job host sized for one CPU and 961 MB, health and the stand-in sites green before and after every step, a game and a lab experiment played under the unit's sandbox with no OOM kill and the memory measured, the stand-in's response time with the lab running within its bound, an upgrade, a failed upgrade that migrated and came back at the old revision, a rollback, a backup restored, the cutover undone; everything cleaned up afterwards. |
| The bump is mechanical | `release.py bump 0.1.6` in a scratch clone; `cargo xtask check`, S7's nextest and goldens and the whole S1 suite green there (5-01, and again at the exit). |
| Nothing was published | No tag, release, PyPI upload, image push or push to another repository; `gh api .../environments` lists exactly `github-pages` and `pypi` (and `helper-signing` once the owner has made it) before and after every dispatch (P5.18.2). |

## P5.2 Versions, the build label and `scripts/release.py`

**One source and a checked set.** Cargo's `[workspace.package] version` is the source: the wheel takes it (`dynamic = ["version"]`), every crate and so every helper takes it, and `cargo xtask check` already holds `citar/__init__.py` to it. Everything else that names the release is a checked set that one script rewrites:

| Place | Rule |
|---|---|
| `Cargo.toml` `[workspace.package] version` | the source |
| `Cargo.lock`, the eleven workspace members (no `source` line); `crates/citar-engine/fuzz/Cargo.lock`, its three path packages | equal (cargo `--locked` refuses otherwise) |
| `citar/__init__.py` `__version__` | equal (also `cargo xtask check`) |
| `installer/citar.iss` `#define AppVersion` | equal (the default for a hand build; CI passes `/DAppVersion` and `/DAppVersionNumeric`) |
| `KNOWN_ISSUES.md` header ("in X") | the source version or newer (it names the release being prepared) |
| `CHANGELOG.md` | `## [Unreleased]` exists; with a version given, `## [V] - YYYY-MM-DD` and the compare links `[V]` and `[Unreleased]` |

The comment in `citar.iss` and the example in `docs/INSTALL.md` become version-free (`X.Y.Z`, `<version>`), so they leave the set. The packaging manifests are not in it: they are written after the release from its files (P5.9).

**`scripts/release.py`** (standard library only, so every job can run it before anything is installed):
- `check [--version V]`: every place in the set agrees (with `--version`, equals V), each disagreement named with its file and line; exit 1 on any.
- `bump V --date YYYY-MM-DD`: refuses V not newer than the source; rewrites the set, the lock's members included; turns `## [Unreleased]` into `## [V] - date` with a new empty `## [Unreleased]` above it and the two compare links; then runs `check --version V`.
- `prepare --mode ci|dry-run|tag [--version V] [--date D]`: the first step of every job that builds or tests (P5.17). `ci` checks; `tag` checks against V and fails on any disagreement; `dry-run` bumps the runner's checkout to V when the source differs (printing a notice that the bump is in this runner only) and then checks. So a dry run of the branch at 0.1.5 builds exactly what a tag of the bumped branch would build, and proves the bump on every runner.
- `label --mode M --version V --sha S`: `v0.1.6` on a tag, `v0.1.6-dryrun+<sha7>` on a dry run, `ci+<sha7>` otherwise. Every release build sets `CITAR_BUILD_ID` to it (wheels, helpers, bundle, image; the sdist's own build sets none, as a user's would not), so `citar doctor`, `build_info()` and `citar-helper --version` name the release.
- `meta`: validates the requested version (`^\d+\.\d+\.\d+(-(alpha|beta|rc)\.\d+)?$`, refused otherwise before any job uses it), and prints the GitHub outputs every job reads: `version`, `pep440` (maturin's mapping: `-rc.1` is `rc1`), `numeric` (Inno's four-part `0.1.6.0`), `label`, `date` (one per run, so jobs that run either side of midnight agree), `mode`, `prerelease`.
- `notes V`: the CHANGELOG section of V, byte for byte (replaces release.yml's awk).
- `assets V [--json]`: the release's asset list (P5.10), the one list every job and check compares with.

**Tests** (`tests/test_release_script.py`): each place planted out of step fails `check` naming it; `bump` in a temporary copy moves every place and nothing else; `prepare`'s three modes; `notes` against the 0.1.5 section; `assets` against P5.10; the regex. test.yml's lint job runs `release.py check`, so a half-done bump fails every pull request.

**As built in 5-01: versions, the build label and `release.py`** (Appendix F, 5-01). One agent, from 2026-10-18 22:44 to 2026-10-19 02:28 (-04:00), on branch `p-5-01` from `5c4bdd7`.

- **What was built.** `scripts/release.py` (standard library only, on Python 3.11 or newer for `tomllib`; an older interpreter is refused with exit 2 and the reason, as the fix round below made it): `check`, `bump`, `prepare`, `label`, `meta`, `notes` and `assets` as above, each taking `--root DIR` (the checkout; by default the script's). The checked set is nineteen places and the changelog: `Cargo.toml`'s source line; the eleven members' entries in `Cargo.lock` (the members' names read from their manifests, so `http-body-util` and `simdutf8`, registry packages that happen to be at 0.1.5, are left alone); the fuzz lock's three; `__version__`; `installer/citar.iss`'s `AppVersion` and the new `AppVersionNumeric` (`X.Y.Z.0`, the prerelease part dropped), with `VersionInfoVersion={#AppVersionNumeric}`; `KNOWN_ISSUES.md`'s header (the source or newer); and `CHANGELOG.md`. Each file keeps its line endings (`citar.iss` is CRLF everywhere). `citar.iss`'s build comment and `docs/INSTALL.md`'s `Get-FileHash` example are version-free. test.yml's lint job runs `release.py check`. CONTRIBUTING's "Releasing" names `bump` and `check`, and says the release workflow is being rebuilt (RELEASING.md is 5-09's). release.yml is untouched: 5-02a reduces it to its gate, and `tests/test_release_script.py` keeps its notes awk as a constant.
- **Decided beyond the text.**
  - The version rule is semver's: the design's regex without leading zeros (cargo refuses them too), matched whole and in ASCII, so `0.1.6\n`, a non-ASCII digit, `0.1.6-rc1` and `v0.1.6` are refused.
  - `check` without `--version` also holds the changelog to the source: one `## [Unreleased]`, above every release; the newest release section the source's, dated; `[Unreleased]` comparing `vSOURCE...HEAD`; `[SOURCE]` comparing from the section below it. A section added by hand for a version not yet bumped fails, naming its line, and a source that is not a version is named at its own line. With `--version V` every place is held to V (KNOWN_ISSUES to V or newer).
  - `bump` runs `check` first and refuses: a tree out of step; a version not newer than the source (semver's order, so `0.1.6-rc.1` comes before `0.1.6`); a date that is not one; an empty Unreleased section, since there is nothing to release (a final release after its `-rc` needs a line there). It prints each place it moves, then checks against V.
  - `notes` stops the last section at the changelog's link definitions, which the awk printed. Only 0.1.0's notes differ, and no release uses them. An empty or missing section is refused.
  - `meta --mode M [--version V] [--sha S] [--date D] [--github-output FILE]`: in `ci` mode the version is the source's. The values go to standard output, and to a file only when the workflow names it (`--github-output "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"`). The script never reads that variable itself, so a test run inside Actions cannot write a step's outputs. `prepare --mode dry-run` prints its notice as a `::notice` workflow command only under `GITHUB_ACTIONS=true`.
  - `assets V [--signed] [--json]` lists each file then its `.sha256`, with `SHA256SUMS` last: 43 files, or 45 with `--signed`, which adds the manifest's `.sig` and its `.sha256`. `--json` adds each asset's group.
  - `label --mode tag` takes no `--sha`.
- **What gate 5 found, and the fixes.** Gate 5 ran the whole suite bumped three times. The first run used the clone at `626e958`, bumped by `prepare --mode dry-run --version 0.1.6`. S1 gave 1,852 run with **4 failures and 9 skips**, against the head's 2 skips. Nextest (1,814 passed, 4 skipped), `golden check` and the jobs set in release (87 passed, 1 skipped) were green. As DESIGN.md P2.2.1 says a version does, the bump moved the build id from `0b412a18fe95` to `db21fcdc3d71` and the `ci` jobs build from `caf48c7f8434` to `ea3249f4dc9d`.
  - `tests/test_helper_conformance.py`'s four job transcripts failed: `job-offer-to-ack`, `job-reconnect-after-send`, `job-result-ack-lost` and `job-duplicate-result`. The hub player sent `<golden:jobs/duel-a/result>` as committed, which names `0b412a18fe95`, and the real hub holds a result to its own build (`job_result_fits`). Every dry run's suite would have failed there.

  - The fix applies the jobs golden set's rule (`GoldenJob::expected`): a result recorded under another build id is held with its build-naming keys set to this build's values.
    - `citar_jobs::rebased` is now bound as `citar._engine.jobs.result_rebased` (with its stub) and exposed as `engine_api.job_result_rebased(kind, spec, result)`. ARCHITECTURE.md names it.

    - Parity compares the rebased record, and skips only when this build plays the game otherwise.

## P5.3 `package.yml` and the wheel matrix

### P5.3.1 One workflow builds what a release ships

`.github/workflows/package.yml` (name `package`) holds every build a release publishes. Triggers: `pull_request`, `push` to `main`, `workflow_dispatch`, and `workflow_call` with the inputs `mode`, `version`, `date` and `label` (release.yml passes them; on its own it computes them in its `meta` job with `mode=ci`). **It publishes nothing**: workflow permissions `contents: read`, no environment, no secret, no registry login (`scripts/ci/check_workflows.py` holds it to that, P5.17). Its concurrency group is `package-${{ github.workflow }}-${{ github.ref }}`, so a run called by release.yml (where `github.workflow` is the caller's name) never shares a group with a push's run and cannot deadlock against its caller or be cancelled by a later push.

Every job starts with the checkout and `release.py prepare` (P5.2), then builds with `CITAR_BUILD_ID` set to the label. Artifacts keep 7 days on pull requests and 14 on dry runs.

**One artifact namespace, shared with test.yml.** When release.yml calls test.yml and package.yml, both upload into the same run, where test.yml's `ext-<os>` wheels carry the test operations and its `helper-<os>` artifacts are dev-guard and test-fault builds. So every artifact package.yml uploads is named `pkg-...` (`pkg-wheel-<platform>`, `pkg-helper-<platform>`, `pkg-release-assets`, ...), release.yml's own are `rel-...`, test.yml's keep their names (none starts with either prefix), and every download in package.yml and release.yml names one artifact exactly or the pattern `pkg-*`/`rel-*` (rule 13, P5.17). Every `uses:` is pinned to a commit SHA with its version in a comment (rule 11); Dependabot's existing `github-actions` updates move the pins.

### P5.3.2 The wheels

`PyO3/maturin-action` (pinned by SHA) at `maturin-version: ${{ env.MATURIN_VERSION }}` (`v1.15.0`, test.yml's pin, moved deliberately), `--release --locked`, the rust-cache keyed per target, each **on its own architecture** (no QEMU, no cross-compilation):

| Platform | Runner | Build | Tag | Library |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| windows-x64 | `windows-latest` | `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` | `cp311-abi3-win_amd64` | `citar/_engine.pyd` |
| macos-arm64 | `macos-15` | `aarch64-apple-darwin`, `MACOSX_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=11.0` | `cp311-abi3-macosx_11_0_arm64` | `citar/_engine.abi3.so` |
| macos-x64 | `macos-15-intel` | `x86_64-apple-darwin`, `MACOSX_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=11.0` | `cp311-abi3-macosx_11_0_x86_64` | `citar/_engine.abi3.so` |
| linux-x64 | `ubuntu-24.04` | `manylinux: 2_28` (the x86_64 container) | `cp311-abi3-manylinux_2_28_x86_64` | `citar/_engine.abi3.so` |
| linux-arm64 | `ubuntu-24.04-arm` | `manylinux: 2_28` (the aarch64 container, native; the job asserts `uname -m` is `aarch64` and passes the native image explicitly if the action would pick a cross image) | `cp311-abi3-manylinux_2_28_aarch64` | `citar/_engine.abi3.so` |

- **abi3 and its floor:** `abi3-py311`, so each wheel serves CPython 3.11 and later; `requires-python = ">=3.11"`.
- **manylinux_2_28** (glibc 2.28, 2018): the VPS (2.39), Debian 12 and 13, Ubuntu 20.04 and later, RHEL 8 and later. maturin's built-in audit refuses a wheel that needs more than its tag says.
- **macOS 11** for both architectures (the arm64 minimum; the x64 build raised to it so both say the same).
- **The label reaches the container** (`docker-options: -e CITAR_BUILD_ID`), and the smoke test (P5.3.4) asserts it arrived.
- Each wheel is checked by `scripts/ci/check_dist.py wheel` with its tag and library (2-06b's checker: exactly the tracked files under `citar/`, the library, the dist-info, PEP 639's license files), then uploaded as `pkg-wheel-<platform>`.

### P5.3.3 The sdist

`maturin sdist` on ubuntu after `prepare`, checked by `check_dist.py sdist`, uploaded as `pkg-sdist`. A second job, `sdist-build`, builds a wheel **from the sdist as a user would** (`pip wheel --no-deps citar-<V>.tar.gz` with the runner's rustup, which honours the sdist's `rust-toolchain.toml`; maturin from build isolation, floating within `<2` as a user's would) and runs the smoke test on it with `--label unknown` (a user's build carries no label); `assemble` holds it to the wheels' version, build id, jobs build and result hash, and to the label `unknown` (P5.10).

### P5.3.4 Each wheel installed and smoke-tested on its own runner

On the runner that built it, for Python **3.11 and 3.14** (the floor and the newest), a fresh venv, `pip install --only-binary=:all: "<wheel>[all]"`, then `scripts/ci/smoke_wheel.py --version V --label L --out smoke-<platform>-<py>.json`, run from a temporary folder with `-P` (2-06b's lesson: never from the checkout), with `CITAR_DATA_DIR` and the other state variables in that folder:
- `citar` and `citar._engine` import from site-packages; `HAS_TEST_OPS` is false; `build_info()` gives V, L and no ruleset folder; the ruleset's counts are sane; the web client, the migrations and the collectors are in the package; the state directory is outside site-packages;
- `citar --version`, `citar where`, `citar doctor --quiet` exit 0;
- **a job host** started through `citar.jobs.host.JobHost` with tiny limits (one thread, 25%, 256 MB) reaches `ready` with `verifiable` true: its canary passed and it replayed the shortest golden job to the committed hashes on this platform (P4.4.3);
- **the same host plays one seeded lab job** (a duel of 30 rounds, the same spec on every platform) through the production path, and its result hash is recorded; then the host is closed;
- the JSON line records the version, label, build id, jobs build, result hash and platform, and is uploaded as `pkg-smoke-<platform>-<py>`.

On linux-x64 under Python 3.12, a third venv installs `"<wheel>[server]"` and writes `pip freeze --exclude citar` to **`citar-<pep440>-server-constraints.txt`** (uploaded as `pkg-constraints`), the exact dependency set the release was tested with: the image and the VPS deploy install with it (P5.7, P5.13).

### P5.3.5 What moves out of test.yml

test.yml's `package`, `installer` and `docker` jobs move into package.yml in 5-02b, onto the release wheels (the installer jobs stop using the ci-profile test wheels), unchanged otherwise until 5-06 and 5-07 replace them; `twine check` moves with `package` and runs over every wheel and the sdist. test.yml keeps the suite and its test-ops wheels (P5.17).

**As built in 5-02b: `package.yml`, the wheel matrix and the sdist** (Appendix F, 5-02b). One agent, from 2026-10-19 12:46 to 15:30 (-04:00), on branch `p-5-02b` from `7313a84`.

- **What was built.**
  - **`.github/workflows/package.yml`** (name `package`): `pull_request`, `push` to `main`, `workflow_dispatch`, and `workflow_call` with `mode`, `version`, `date` and `label`; the group `package-${{ github.workflow }}-${{ github.ref }}`; `contents: read`, no environment, no secret; every action pinned as test.yml pins it; every checkout `persist-credentials: false`; every job a `timeout-minutes`. Its jobs:
    - `meta` (ubuntu-24.04): `release.py prepare`, then `release.py meta --mode M [--version V] --sha "$GITHUB_SHA" [--date D] --github-output "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"`, whose seven values (`version`, `pep440`, `numeric`, `label`, `date`, `mode`, `prerelease`) are the job's outputs. Every other job takes `RELEASE_MODE`, `RELEASE_VERSION`, `RELEASE_DATE` (and `PEP440` where it checks a file) from them, so one date serves the whole run.
    - `wheels`: P5.3.2's five legs (`windows-latest`, `macos-15`, `macos-15-intel`, `ubuntu-24.04`, `ubuntu-24.04-arm`), each with its target, tag, library, `MACOSX_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=11.0` on the Macs, and `CITAR_BUILD_ID` at the job level from `meta`'s label. Each leg sets up Python 3.11 and 3.14 (`update-environment: false`, for the smoke) and 3.12 (the build's), prepares, asserts `uname -m` is its own architecture, installs `rust-toolchain.toml`'s toolchain and restores the rust-cache (`wheel-<target>`), both on the native legs only, builds with maturin-action (`maturin-version: ${{ env.MATURIN_VERSION }}`, `--release --locked --out dist`, `target`, `manylinux`, `docker-options: -e CITAR_BUILD_ID`, and on Linux the native container named outright and pinned by digest: `quay.io/pypa/manylinux_2_28_x86_64@sha256:1e7b8460...` and `..._aarch64@sha256:b873ffc3...`, PyPA's `2026.10.16-1`), runs `check_dist.py wheel dist --tag T --library L --version P` and `twine check`, then `smoke_wheel.py smoke` on 3.11 and on 3.14 in that job, and uploads `pkg-wheel-<platform>`, `pkg-smoke-<platform>-3.11` and `-3.14`.
    - `sdist`: `maturin sdist` after `rustup toolchain install`, `check_dist.py sdist --version P`, `twine check`, `pkg-sdist`.
    - `sdist-build`: `pip wheel --no-deps` of the downloaded sdist from a folder outside the checkout, with no `CITAR_BUILD_ID` anywhere in the job (and `env -u` on the command), the runner's rustup and maturin from build isolation; `check_dist.py wheel built --library citar/_engine.abi3.so --version P` (no tag: pip's build is a plain `linux_x86_64` one); `smoke_wheel.py smoke --label unknown` on 3.12; `pkg-smoke-sdist`.
    - `constraints`: `smoke_wheel.py constraints` over `pkg-wheel-linux-x64` on Python 3.12, `citar-<pep440>-server-constraints.txt`, `pkg-constraints`.
    - `installer` (`linux-x64` on `ubuntu-24.04`, `macos-arm64` on `macos-15`) and `docker`: test.yml's jobs, moved, on `pkg-wheel-<platform>` and `pkg-wheel-linux-x64` (into `wheelhouse/`); otherwise unchanged until 5-06 and 5-07.
  - **`scripts/ci/smoke_wheel.py`** (standard library; `smoke`, `constraints`, and the internal `inside`): `smoke` makes a fresh venv with the given interpreter (every folder it clears, the venv, `WORK/state` and `WORK/run`, is checked before any is touched: missing, empty, or marked `.smoke_wheel` by an earlier run; never a link, never a venv holding an interpreter the run uses; see the fix round), installs `"<wheel>[all]"` with `--only-binary=:all:`, and runs `inside` with the venv's Python as `python -P smoke_wheel.py inside ...` from `WORK/run`, every `CITAR_*` variable dropped (and `PYTHONPATH`, `PYTHONHOME`, `VIRTUAL_ENV`) and the five state variables (`CITAR_STATE_DIR`, `_DATA_DIR`, `_SAVE_DIR`, `_CONFIG_DIR`, `_BENCH_DIR`) under `WORK/state`. `inside` checks P5.3.4's list and writes one JSON line: `smoke`, `platform`, `python`, `wheel`, `version`, `pep440`, `label`, `build_id`, `jobs_build`, `rules`, `spec_hash`, `result_hash`, `turns`, `verifiable`, `host_limits`, `seconds`. `constraints` installs `"<wheel>[server]"` the same way and writes `pip freeze --exclude citar`, refusing an empty set, a line that pins nothing from an index (`name @ file://`, `-e`), CITAR itself, or a distribution twice.
  - **`scripts/ci/check_dist.py`**: `--version P` for both kinds (the file name's version, and the version `METADATA` or `PKG-INFO` states, which must always be the file name's), and PEP 639: each of `[project]`'s `license-files` under the wheel's `<dist-info>/licenses/` or at the sdist's top, `License-File` naming exactly them and `License-Expression` equal to `[project]`'s `license`. The command line refuses (exit 2) a `pyproject.toml` without both.
  - **test.yml** loses `package`, `installer` and `docker`; its header and build-ext's comments say package.yml builds the release wheels.
  - **`cargo xtask check`**: the helper rule reads `package.yml` whole, as it reads release.yml (`helper::WORKFLOWS` lists the three files and how each is read, and `reads()` takes them from there, so the triggers rule asks rust.yml's filters for `package.yml`); rust.yml's `push` and `pull_request` filters list it.
  - **Tests.** `tests/test_ci_scripts.py` (32 tests, 21 before): check_dist's version and licence checks and `metadata_fields`, and smoke_wheel's parts that need no built wheel. `tests/test_workflows.py` (66, 58 before): `ThePackageWorkflow` holds the real file to P5.3 where no rule does (the triggers, inputs, group and permissions; one `MATURIN_VERSION` in both workflows; every job's values from `meta`; P5.3.2's table, matched to `release.py`'s `WHEEL_TAGS` and `HELPER_PLATFORMS`; the label, the build's arguments, `check_dist`, `twine`, the architecture check and the smoke on both interpreters; the sdist, its build without a label and the constraints; the moved jobs) and plants a finding of rules 3, 5, 9 and 13 in the real file; the plants that named test.yml's `package` job now name `build-ext`. xtask: `package_yml_is_read_whole`, and the triggers test fails without package.yml's filter lines.
  - CONTRIBUTING's "The extension in CI" and the Dockerfile's comment name package.yml; the wiki is regenerated (never pushed).
- **Decided beyond the text.**
  - **The smoke runs in the `wheels` job itself,** after the build, on 3.11 then 3.14 ("on the runner that built it", literally): no second set of ten jobs, and no extra macOS runners to wait for (5-02a's merge saw a macOS job never acquired).
  - **`constraints` is a job of its own,** not a step of the linux-x64 leg: rule 13 counts an upload once per matrix leg whatever the step's `if:`, so a `pkg-constraints` step in the matrix would be five uploads of one name.
  - **The Linux containers are named,** both of them, rather than left to maturin-action's table (which today picks the same native images): a change of its defaults cannot swap in a cross-compiling image, and the job's `uname -m` check proves the runner's architecture on every leg.
  - **The rust-cache is for the native builds only:** a container's build runs as root with a cargo home of its own, so the runner can neither restore nor save it.
  - **`meta` checks a caller's label:** when `label` is passed, it must be the one `release.py meta` computes here, or the job fails.
  - **The installer's macOS leg is `macos-15`,** the runner the `macos-arm64` wheel is built on (test.yml's was `macos-latest`).
  - **`installer`, `docker` and `constraints` run when `meta` passed and nothing was cancelled** (`!cancelled() && needs.meta.result == 'success'`), so one platform's failed wheel does not skip the others' checks; each then fails at its own download if its wheel is missing.
  - **The helper rule also refuses `test-ops`** (citar-py's test operations) on every line it reads: release.yml, package.yml and helper.yml's artifact jobs build what ships.
  - **The smoke records more than the design's six fields** (the spec hash, the ruleset id, the turns, the host's limits, the wheel's name, the time), checks that the result answers its spec (`job_result_fits`) and names this build, that `citar.__version__` and the installed distribution's version are V and its PEP 440 form, and that with the state variables unset the default state directory would still be outside site-packages (computed, nothing created).
  - **The sdist for gate 3 was built in WSL** in the maturin container, as the `sdist` job builds it on Linux; one built on Windows also passed `check_dist.py sdist` and `twine check`.
- **What the gates found, and the fix.** The first S1 (at `d71f064`) failed one test: `test_engine_boundary` holds every module and script to the one door, `citar.engine_api`, and smoke_wheel.py imported `citar._engine` for the library's file and `HAS_TEST_OPS`. It now asks the extension those two things in a fresh interpreter of the venv with `-P`, as `unpack_ext.py` does, and everything else goes through `engine_api`; the checks also assert that they run with `-P` (`sys.flags.safe_path`). Gates 1 to 3 and the suites were run again at the fix (`0b0f2d4`).

- **Fix round** (2026-10-19, 16:07 to 17:41 (-04:00), one agent; the review found four minor issues, all valid, all fixed; commits `f465d36`, `0f37ea0`, `59034ad`, and this note).
  1. **`smoke_wheel.py` cleared folders it had not made.** `smoke` deleted `WORK/state` and `WORK/run` before any check, and `fresh_venv` ran `venv --clear` on any folder holding `pyvenv.cfg`, the venv running the script included. Reproduced at `fa740aa`: a `--work` folder's `state/precious.txt` and `run/notes.txt` were gone before a missing `--python` failed the run (`fix-finding1-old.log`). Now `claim()` checks every folder a run clears before any is touched. Each must be missing, empty, or marked `.smoke_wheel` by an earlier run, and never a link (a symbolic link or a junction; a link above the folder, like macOS's `/tmp`, is fine). The venv must hold neither the interpreter running the script nor `--python`'s (`sys.prefix` and `sys.base_prefix`, asked with `-I`), and must not overlap `WORK/state` or `WORK/run`. A `--python` that does not run is a usage error. Each refusal exits 2 with nothing cleared. `renew()` empties a claimed folder and marks it; the venv is made with `python -m venv` inside its marked folder (no `--clear`), so a failed creation leaves a folder a rerun may clear. The reviewer's cases, the package's own venv as `--venv` and a venv the old version made all exit 2 with every file in place (`fix-finding1.log`). `test_ci_scripts` has 36 tests (32 before): a refused run clears nothing, the venv is never an interpreter the run uses, links are refused, and empty or marked folders are renewed.
  2. **The Linux wheels' images were moving tags.** Both legs now name PyPA's `2026.10.16-1` by digest, `latest` on 2026-10-19: `quay.io/pypa/manylinux_2_28_x86_64@sha256:1e7b8460ad8fb3fb0fbe1cd818845159ac896b4fd17452d56e892c291f3b97a8` and `quay.io/pypa/manylinux_2_28_aarch64@sha256:b873ffc316ffe8a154e3dc4872871de2ebd170db4876c8bbd983d42ff320001e`. Each was read from quay.io's registry (read only) as a single linux/amd64 or linux/arm64 manifest. They are moved deliberately, like the image's base (P5.7); Dependabot does not read them, and CONTRIBUTING says how to move one. **Rule 11 now covers every image a workflow builds or runs in** (its text in P5.17 above): a job's `container:` and `services:`, and maturin-action's `container:`, each matrix leg's. maturin-action is read as it decides (`innerMain` at the pinned v1.51.0): only on Linux, only for `build` and `publish`; `container: off` builds on the runner; with no container named (or `on`, `auto`, `true`), a `manylinux` that asks for a container takes the action's own image by a moving tag, and fails. `publish` and a cross-compiled Linux target default to `auto`; the target is read from `target:` or from `--target` in `args`. A `docker run` in a script is not read, since a script's words name no image reliably. `test_workflows` has 68 tests (66 before). Gate 2's extra rehearsal, below, built in the pinned x86_64 image.
  3. **Gate 5 was reported as passed.** It is CI-only and is pending at the merge push. The report now names it as not passed for that reason, and its other gates are the local ones.
  4. **The wheels job did not install the toolchain before the cache.** It now runs `rustup toolchain install` after prepare and before Swatinem/rust-cache on the native legs, as test.yml's `build-ext` does, so the cache's key never depends on rustup installing the toolchain on first use. `ThePackageWorkflow` holds the order.
  - **Found while rerunning gate 4: `cargo xtask check` ran another checkout's xtask.** The review had built xtask from its own tree (`C:/dev/target/citar-p-5-02b/review/tree`) into this package's `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` at 15:37. The worktree's own xtask unit stayed fresh, even after `touch xtask/src/main.rs`, so cargo never linked it back over `debug/xtask.exe`. The first rerun's plants therefore passed against the review tree's files. `cargo clean -p xtask` rebuilt it from the worktree (its path is now the one in the binary), and gate 4 then failed each plant as it should. The first round's gate 4, S7 and S8 ran before the review's build, and their logs show the plants caught. Lesson: never let two checkouts share a `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` for `cargo xtask`, whose root is fixed at build time.

## P5.4 The helper's release builds and the signing path

### P5.4.1 Ten builds on five runners

package.yml's `helpers` matrix is P4.10's targets and unversioned names (helper.yml's `artifact` matrix stays equal to it and `helper.test.mjs` reads both). **The Linux builds move into the manylinux_2_28 containers**, natively (`quay.io/pypa/manylinux_2_28_x86_64` on `ubuntu-24.04`, `..._aarch64` on `ubuntu-24.04-arm`, the toolchain installed in the container from `rust-toolchain.toml` by a `rustup-init` pinned by version and SHA-256), so the helper's glibc floor is the game's, 2.28: the helper uses rustls on ring (`Cargo.toml`: no OpenSSL, no CMake), so the container's gcc toolset is all it needs. After each Linux build the job reads the binary's dynamic symbols (`objdump -T`) and fails if any `GLIBC_` version is newer than 2.28. helper.yml's `artifact` jobs build the same way, so a pull request catches a floor regression. On each runner, `cargo build --release --locked -p citar-helper --target T` with `CITAR_BUILD_ID` and, on macOS, `MACOSX_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=11.0`, twice:
- **the self-updating build** (no features): copied to its unversioned name, with its `.sha256`; what the Servers page links (`releases/latest/download/<name>`), `install.sh --worker` installs, and the manifest lists;
- **the packaged build** (`--features packaged`, a literal feature list, which `cargo xtask check` allows: `packaged` is no test feature): packed into a **versioned archive**, `citar-helper-<V>-<platform>.tar.gz` (`.zip` on Windows), holding `citar-helper[.exe]`, `LICENSE`, `NOTICE.md` and `THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.txt`, with its `.sha256`. Homebrew, Scoop and winget install these (P5.9); they never self-update (P4.3.8).

Both builds are run on their runner (outside the container, on the runner's newer glibc, as a user's box would): `--version` must name the target, the version V and the label; `--self-test` passes; the packaged one's `update check` reports a packaged build with policy `notify`. Every run passes `--home` under `$RUNNER_TEMP` (a release build has no development guard, so without it the helper's home is the runner user's real per-user base). The Fedora TLS check (4-14) stays on linux-x64. The two builds share every crate but `citar-helper` itself, so the second costs one crate and the link. The self-updating builds upload as `pkg-helper-<platform>`, the archives as `pkg-helper-packaged-<platform>`.

### P5.4.2 The manifest and a signature that proves the path without the key

package.yml's `helper-manifest` job (`ubuntu-24.04`, no environment, no secret) downloads the five self-updating builds, builds xtask once (`cargo build --release --locked -p xtask`; on a dry run or a tag from the locked sources alone, no cache restored), runs **that binary** for every step below (so the binary it uploads is the one these steps proved), and:
1. writes `citar-helper-manifest.json` with `xtask helper-manifest --assets DIR --version V` (it runs the Linux build, so it runs on Linux);
2. makes a **throwaway key** with `xtask keygen --out "$RUNNER_TEMP/throwaway"` (outside the checkout, which keygen insists on), signs with `xtask sign-manifest --key ...`, and verifies the signature twice: with a new **`xtask verify-manifest --pub FILE MANIFEST [SIG]`** (5-03; ed25519 over `CITAR-HELPER-MANIFEST-V1\0` and the bytes as published, the same domain citar-jobs' test holds equal) and with an independent verifier, `scripts/ci/verify_manifest.py` (the `cryptography` package's Ed25519), so the format helpers check is checked by code that shares nothing with the signer; a copy with one byte changed must fail both;
3. checks that a release build with the committed `update-keys.pub` behaves as the tree says: empty, `update check` reports updates unavailable; not empty, each line is a well-formed public key;
4. uploads `pkg-helper-manifest` (the manifest alone, which the release attaches), `pkg-helper-manifest-dry-run` (the throwaway signature and public key, named so, never a release asset) and **`pkg-xtask-linux-x64`**, the xtask binary this job built and used, with its SHA-256: the tool release.yml's signing job runs on the same runner image, so that job builds nothing. The private half stays in `$RUNNER_TEMP` and dies with the runner; `check_workflows.py` refuses an upload path that could include it.

**The real signature is release.yml's alone** (P5.11): a tag-only job in the environment `helper-signing`, under its required reviewer, signs the manifest package.yml built, then verifies the signature against each line of the committed `update-keys.pub`. **The key never meets a build or an installer.** The job's environment is the tag expression (P5.11), so no other run can name it. It runs on `ubuntu-24.04`, the image the xtask binary was built on, and its steps are only: `actions/checkout` (pinned, `persist-credentials: false`) for `update-keys.pub` and `scripts/ci/release_sign.py`; `actions/download-artifact` (pinned) for `pkg-helper-manifest` and `pkg-xtask-linux-x64`, whose hash is checked against the one beside it; **one step whose only `env:` is the secret**, which runs `python3 scripts/ci/release_sign.py` on the runner's own Python (standard library only), and that script runs the downloaded xtask offline (`sign-manifest --key-env`, then `verify-manifest --pub` against each committed line); and `actions/upload-artifact` (pinned) for `rel-helper-signature`. No cargo, pip, npm or setup action runs anywhere in that job, and no other action, because a process an earlier step leaves behind can read a later step's environment (rule 15, P5.17). The independent verifier (`verify_manifest.py`, which needs `cryptography`) runs in the next job, `check-signature`, which holds no secret. The outcomes:

| `update-keys.pub` | Secret | Outcome |
|---|---|---|
| empty | absent | a notice; no `.sig`; the release's helpers report updates unavailable (the tree's state today) |
| empty | present | **fail**: "commit the public key first" (the signature would be trusted by nobody) |
| a key | absent | **fail**: helpers would trust a key no release signs with |
| a key | present | sign; the signature must verify against a committed key, else **fail** ("the secret is not the committed key") |

A dry run skips this job, and **by construction never names its environment**: the job's `environment:` is `${{ github.event_name == 'push' && startsWith(github.ref, 'refs/tags/v') && 'helper-signing' || '' }}`, which is empty on a dispatch, so whether a skipped job would create a literal environment no longer matters, and nobody is waited for. package.yml has proved everything up to the signature with the throwaway key, and `scripts/ci/release_sign.py` (5-09), which holds the four rules, is tested locally with throwaway keys and the xtask binary.

**As built in 5-03: the helper's release builds and the signing path** (Appendix F, 5-03). One agent, from 2026-10-19 21:04 to 23:35 (-04:00), on branch `p-5-03` from `ff99091`.

- **What was built.**
  - **package.yml's `helpers`** (five legs, one line a target: `platform`, `runner`, `machine`, `target`, `asset`, `container`): each on the wheel's own runner and architecture, `CITAR_BUILD_ID` the label and `MACOSX_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=11.0` at the job level. After prepare and the `uname -m` check, the Windows and macOS legs install the toolchain, restore the rust-cache (`pkg-helper-<target>`) and build on the runner: `cargo build --release --locked -p citar-helper --target "$TARGET"`, copied to `build/self/`, then the same with `--features packaged`, copied to `build/packaged/` (one crate and the link more). The Linux legs run the same two builds in the wheels' manylinux_2_28 images, by the same digests, through `docker run` with the label passed in by name, rustup and `rust-toolchain.toml`'s toolchain installed in the image, and the outputs given back to the runner's user. Then:
    - `scripts/ci/helper_release.py glibc --max 2.28` on both Linux builds (`objdump -T`'s `GLIBC_` versions, refused above the floor, or a private or no glibc version);
    - `stage`: the self-updating build as `citar-helper-<platform>` (`.exe` on Windows) and the packaged one in `citar-helper-<V>-<platform>.tar.gz` (`.zip` on Windows), flat, holding the binary (0755), `LICENSE` and `NOTICE.md`, reproducible (a fixed order, 1980-01-01, owner 0, gzip without a name or time), each file with its `.sha256` (the hex digest and a newline), the listing then checked;
    - `check`, on the runner and outside any container, every run through `gate_run.py helper` with a home of its own under `$RUNNER_TEMP`: `--version` must be exactly `citar-helper V (build B, jobs build J, protocol P, target T, L)`; `--self-test`; `update check` must say `update keys trusted: N` for the tree's `update-keys.pub` and, with none, `updates: unavailable, this build trusts no update key; policy: auto` (the packaged one: `notify (a packaged build: its package manager updates it)`); the packaged binary, unpacked from its archive, with `"updates": "auto"` in its `helper.json` says `auto, which a packaged build refuses: update it through your package manager`, and its `update apply` exits 2; both builds report one build id and one jobs build. It writes `report/helper-<platform>.json` (both lines, hashes, sizes, runs);
    - on linux-x64, 4-14's Fedora TLS check, the image now `docker.io/library/fedora@sha256:be9d65e2...` (Docker Hub's official Fedora 44, linux/amd64, pushed 2026-08-26; 5-03 first pinned `quay.io/fedora/fedora@sha256:0db60e5a...`, `latest` on 2026-10-19, but quay.io/fedora/fedora rebuilds its tags daily and deletes the day before's manifests, so at 5-04's merge push the pull failed with "manifest unknown"), which first runs the build's `--version` on Fedora's glibc; every run with `--home`;
    - uploads `pkg-helper-<platform>` (the binary and its `.sha256`), `pkg-helper-packaged-<platform>` (the archive and its `.sha256`) and `pkg-helper-report-<platform>`.
  - **package.yml's `helper-manifest`** (`ubuntu-24.04`, needs `meta` and `helpers`, no environment, no secret): the five `pkg-helper-<platform>` by name into `assets/`; `cargo build --release --locked -p xtask` once, copied to `xtask-bin/xtask` with its `.sha256`, and that binary runs every step: `helper-manifest --assets assets --version V` (the build and protocol read from the Linux x64 build), read back by `helper_release.py manifest` (version V, five targets, each name, URL in the release's download folder, SHA-256 and size equal to the file's and to its runner's `.sha256`); `keygen --out "$RUNNER_TEMP/throwaway"`, `sign-manifest --key ...`, `verify-manifest --pub` and `verify_manifest.py --pub` with the throwaway key's public half; a copy with one byte changed and a second throwaway key each refused by both (exit 1, not 2); `verify_manifest.py --check-keys crates/citar-jobs/update-keys.pub` and the Linux build's `update check` through `gate_run.py` (`update keys trusted: N`; with none, `updates: unavailable`). Uploads `pkg-helper-manifest` (the manifest alone), `pkg-helper-manifest-dry-run` (`citar-helper-manifest.json.sig` and `dry-run-signing.pub`, named one by one) and `pkg-xtask-linux-x64` (`xtask` and `xtask.sha256`).
  - **helper.yml's artifact matrix** is package.yml's, line for line (target, name, runner, image): the Linux legs moved from `ubuntu-22.04(-arm)` to `ubuntu-24.04(-arm)` and build in the containers, with the same glibc check; every run (`--version`, `--self-test`, `service install --dry-run`) takes `--home "$RUNNER_TEMP/citar-dry-run"`; `MACOSX_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=11.0`; its path filters list `helper_release.py` and `gate_run.py`.
  - **`cargo xtask verify-manifest --pub FILE MANIFEST [SIG]`** (`SIG` by default `MANIFEST.sig`): each key of `FILE` read as `update-keys.pub` is (blank and `#` lines past, a malformed line refused by its number and never echoed, none refused), the signature checked with `verify_strict` over the domain and the bytes as published, the key's line named; then the signed file must be one a helper reads (at most 65,536 bytes, `v` 1, some assets). Exit 0, 1 (does not verify, or no helper reads it) or 2 (refused). `helper-manifest`'s probe now runs each asset's `--version` with `--home` (a folder of its own that does not exist), since a release build has no development guard.
  - **`scripts/ci/verify_manifest.py`** (the `cryptography` package's Ed25519, which uses OpenSSL and shares no code with `ed25519-dalek`): the same check and exit codes; `--check-keys FILE` (each line a public key, at most two). **`tests/data/helper_manifest/`** holds a manifest, its signature and its public key, made by `cryptography` from the seed `[9; 32]` (no file holds it): xtask's `the_committed_fixture_is_this_signers_signature_and_verifies` re-signs it with dalek and gets the same signature byte for byte, and the Python suite verifies it. Kept byte for byte (`-text`).
  - **`scripts/ci/gate_run.py`** (P5.18.1 rules 2 and 11): `helper [--under DIR] [--timeout S] -- BINARY ARGS` and `server --port N --state DIR [--under DIR] [--env NAME=VALUE]... [--ready PATH] [--stop-file FILE] [--timeout S] -- COMMAND ARGS`, as its docstring says. `--under` defaults to `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` and must overlap neither the checkout nor the per-user CITAR folder.
  - **The helper's `update check`** reads its policy alone (`config::updates`: the flag, the settings file, the build's default), so a home with no `helper.json`, as a package manager leaves it, still says it, where it said `unknown (Give --server ...)`; its `updates:` line says `unavailable` when the build trusts no key, a packaged build's `notify`, and that a packaged build refuses `auto` (the run's warning said so; `update check` read as if `auto` would install).
  - **check_workflows rule 11** also reads an image a script runs, where it is written: a matrix leg's `container` or `image`, or a matrix or `env:` key ending in either (`FEDORA_IMAGE`), each value that is not an expression or one of maturin-action's switches pinned by digest.
  - **helper.test.mjs** reads package.yml's `helpers` and helper.yml's `artifact` (the reader now takes a value with colons, an image's digest, and quotes): five each, the card's names, one build a target, the Linux images by digest on their own architecture, the two equal line for line, and the manifest's table equal.
  - **Tests**: `tests/test_gate_run.py` (13), `tests/test_helper_release.py` (19; `Check` runs a stand-in helper script on Unix only), `tests/test_workflows.py` (72, 68 before: the helpers, the manifest job, helper.yml's artifacts, and rule 11's named images; plants of rules 3, 9, 11 and 13 in the new jobs), xtask (3 new: 8 in `manifest.rs`), citar-helper (2 new). CONTRIBUTING ("The helper's release builds", "Running a release helper or a server on your own machine") and WORKERS.md's step 4; the wiki is regenerated (never pushed).
- **Decided beyond the text.**
  - **One script for the helpers' steps,** `scripts/ci/helper_release.py` (the design names the checks, not where they live), so the CI steps and the local gates run the same code, and each part has tests.
  - **The Linux builds run in the image through `docker run` in a step,** not maturin-action and not a job's `container:`: the binaries must then run on the runner, outside the image, and a job container would hold the whole job. Rule 11 could not see the image a script runs, so the image is written in the matrix's `container` and rule 11 now reads such keys (and `*_IMAGE` in `env:`); tests/test_workflows.py and helper.test.mjs hold the helpers' and helper.yml's images equal to the wheels'.
  - **A report per platform,** `pkg-helper-report-<platform>`, for 5-08's `assemble`, which holds the ten helpers to one version, label, build and jobs build but cannot run a Windows or macOS binary on Linux: each runner records what its builds said.
  - **The packaged binary checked is the one unpacked from its archive,** so the archive's binary is proved executable as a user unpacks it.
  - **`helper-manifest` reads its manifest back** against the five files and the hashes their own runners wrote (`helper_release.py manifest`), and checks that the negatives fail with exit 1 (does not verify), not 2 (a refusal that proves nothing).
  - **The dry run's public key is uploaded as `dry-run-signing.pub`:** rule 9 refuses any path with a `throwaway` part, and the name says what it is.
  - **`update check` changed** (the helper, not the jobs crate: the jobs build is unchanged): P5.4.2 asks it to report updates unavailable, and it printed the key count and the policy; P5.4.1 asks the packaged build to report `notify`, and with no `helper.json` it printed `unknown (Give --server ...)`, the state a package manager installs it in.
  - **gate_run.py refuses an abbreviation** of the options it reads (the helper takes any unambiguous prefix, `--hom` for `--home`), refuses `--detach`, `--supervisor` and a service's install, uninstall or run without `--dry-run`, and points the per-user base (`LOCALAPPDATA`, `XDG_DATA_HOME`) inside `--under` as well, so a fallback the flag missed lands there too. The server's whole process tree is recorded before the stop (an orphan on Unix is given another parent), stopped politely and then killed, and checked gone.
  - **The independent verifier's floor** is pyproject's `cryptography>=42.0` (the `keyring` extra's), installed in the job, as every suite environment has it.
  - **The archive is flat** (no top folder): Homebrew, Scoop and winget each name the binary inside it.
  - **gate_run.py, hardened by the real server's rehearsal:** Git Bash rewrote `--ready /api/auth/config` into `C:/Program Files/Git/api/auth/config`, the readiness request raised, and gate_run died while the server was starting (it had exited by itself, for want of its secret key, so nothing was left). Now `--ready` is refused unless it is a URL's path (naming `MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=1`), any failure in the wait loop still stops the whole tree, and a helper run interrupted for any reason is stopped too; two tests hold it.
- **Gates** (logs under `C:/dev/target/citar-p-5-03/logs`; the scripts are in the session's scratchpad `p503/`):
  1. **Windows** (`gate1-build.log`, `gate1.log`, `../gate1/`): both `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` builds in `release` from `857e87a` (the Rust's head) with `CITAR_BUILD_ID=ci+857e87a`, as the helpers job's native step makes them: the self-updating build in 408 s from a cold target, the packaged one in 222 s more (only `citar-helper` and its link, fat LTO). `helper_release.py stage` gave `citar-helper-windows-x64.exe` (16,737,280 bytes) and `citar-helper-0.1.5-windows-x64.zip` (6,590,005 bytes: `citar-helper.exe` 755, `LICENSE`, `NOTICE.md`) with their `.sha256`. `helper_release.py check` ran both through `gate_run.py helper`, each with a home under the target (2.6 s): `--version` is exactly `citar-helper 0.1.5 (build 0b412a18fe95, jobs build b2a9ff8212e2, protocol 2, target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc, ci+857e87a)` for both; `--self-test` passes (canary ok; self-check duel-a 30 rounds, result `16f1a8f0...`); `update check` says `update keys trusted: 0` and `updates: unavailable, this build trusts no update key; policy: auto` (the packaged one: `policy: notify (a packaged build: its package manager updates it)`); with `"updates": "auto"` in its `helper.json` the packaged build says `auto, which a packaged build refuses: update it through your package manager`, and its `update apply` exits 2 ("installed by a package manager").
