## P5.10 Assembling a release

**The asset list** (`release.py assets V`; `P` is V's PEP 440 form; every file but the `.sha256` files and `SHA256SUMS` has a `.sha256` beside it):

| Group | Files |
|---|---|
| Python | `citar-P.tar.gz`; `citar-P-cp311-abi3-win_amd64.whl`; `citar-P-cp311-abi3-macosx_11_0_arm64.whl`; `citar-P-cp311-abi3-macosx_11_0_x86_64.whl`; `citar-P-cp311-abi3-manylinux_2_28_x86_64.whl`; `citar-P-cp311-abi3-manylinux_2_28_aarch64.whl`; `citar-P-server-constraints.txt` |
| Windows | `CITAR-V-setup.exe`; `CITAR-V-windows-x64.zip` |
| The helper, self-updating (unversioned, P4.10) | `citar-helper-windows-x64.exe`; `citar-helper-macos-arm64`; `citar-helper-macos-x64`; `citar-helper-linux-x64`; `citar-helper-linux-arm64`; `citar-helper-manifest.json` (and `citar-helper-manifest.json.sig` on a signed tag) |
| The helper, packaged | `citar-helper-V-windows-x64.zip`; `citar-helper-V-macos-arm64.tar.gz`; `citar-helper-V-macos-x64.tar.gz`; `citar-helper-V-linux-x64.tar.gz`; `citar-helper-V-linux-arm64.tar.gz` |
| Notices and sums | `THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.txt`; `SHA256SUMS` |

**package.yml's `assemble` job** (needs every build) downloads the `pkg-` artifacts it needs by exact name into `release/` under exactly these names and fails on a missing or an extra file, naming it (`scripts/ci/assemble_release.py`). It then checks that **every artifact is one build**: the five wheels' smoke lines, the ten helpers' `--version` lines, the bundle's and both images' report one version V, one label L, one build id and one jobs build (so the VPS's job host and every released helper can share CPU work, P4.4.5), and the five wheels played the seeded lab job to one result hash; the sdist build's line must report V, the same build id, jobs build and result hash, and the label `unknown` (a user's build has none). It runs `twine check` over the wheels and the sdist, writes `SHA256SUMS`, the **release notes** (`release.py notes V`, then the checksums, `gh attestation verify` instructions and the install routes), the attestation subject list (every file of `release/`), and the package managers' manifests from the files (`release_checksums.py --from-dir release --out managers`, the real release URLs). It uploads `pkg-release-assets` (every file), `pkg-python-dists` (the wheels and the sdist, for PyPI) and `pkg-release-preview` (notes, sums, manifests, the subject list, the asset list with sizes). The `managers` job (P5.9) uses `pkg-release-assets` behind a loopback server.

---

## P5.11 release.yml end to end

**The Phase 5 gate goes.** release.yml keeps its triggers (`push` of `v*` tags; `workflow_dispatch` with `version`), workflow permissions `contents: read`, and gains `concurrency: release-${{ github.ref }}` with `cancel-in-progress: false` (a release is never cut off mid-publish). **It builds nothing itself**: every build is package.yml's, every test test.yml's, both called, so a release can contain nothing a pull request did not build and test. The two calling jobs pass **no `secrets:`** (never `secrets: inherit`, which would hand every called job the repository's secrets, `WIKI_TOKEN` among them) and set `permissions: contents: read` (rule 12).

**The tag guard.** Every job that publishes, names an environment, holds a secret or writes anywhere has the job-level condition `github.event_name == 'push' && startsWith(github.ref, 'refs/tags/v')` (a dispatch on a tag ref is a dry run too), never under `always()` or `!cancelled()`. `check_workflows.py` proves it before any dispatch (P5.17).

**Environments by construction.** A job's `if:` is evaluated at run time, and whether GitHub creates a missing environment for a job it then skips is platform behaviour nobody here can check without risking exactly that. So every environment in release.yml except `pypi` (which exists, and which PyPI's trusted publisher names) is written as the tag expression, `environment: ${{ github.event_name == 'push' && startsWith(github.ref, 'refs/tags/v') && 'helper-signing' || '' }}`: on a dispatch it is empty and names nothing, whatever the `if:` does. `check_workflows.py` requires that form (rule 14), with a planted literal `environment: helper-signing` that must fail. **If Actions refuses an empty environment name** (the first dry run, 5-09, shows it as a startup failure, which names nothing and creates nothing), the fallback is to split the paths: release.yml keeps only `push: tags` (so the publishing code stays where PyPI's trusted publisher expects it), and a dispatch-only `release-dry-run.yml` calls the same test.yml, package.yml and summary and names no environment at all; the dispatch command in P5.18.2 then names that file.

**Nothing in a job that holds power installs or builds.** A job that reads a secret other than `GITHUB_TOKEN`, or holds `id-token: write` or any write permission, runs no cargo, pip, npm, maturin or `docker build`, and its `uses:` are on rule 15's allow-list (the pinned checkout, download and upload actions, and the publishing actions the job exists for); its checkouts set `persist-credentials: false` (rule 15). A step in any workflow that reads such a secret runs no package tool and is not a `uses:` step. So the PyPI job does no `twine check` (assemble did) and the signing job runs a prebuilt binary (P5.4.2).

| Job | Needs | On a tag | On a dry run | Writes / holds |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `meta` | | `release.py meta` with the tag; `prepare --mode tag` (the tag, the source and the changelog agree) | `meta` with the input; `prepare --mode dry-run` | nothing |
| `tests` (calls test.yml) | `meta` | yes | yes | nothing |
| `package` (calls package.yml) | `meta` | yes | yes | nothing |
| `sign-helper-manifest` | `tests`, `package` | yes: environment by the tag expression, `helper-signing` (its reviewer approves); P5.4.2's four rules by the prebuilt xtask; uploads `rel-helper-signature` | skipped | the secret |
| `check-signature` | `sign-helper-manifest` | yes: `verify_manifest.py` (`cryptography` installed from hashes) against each committed key line; with an empty `update-keys.pub`, that no `.sig` exists | skipped | nothing |
| `attest-files` | `check-signature` | yes: `actions/attest-build-provenance` over every file of `pkg-release-assets` and the signature (the subject list `assemble` wrote). It publishes provenance only, so a first-run failure here stops the release before anything irreversible | skipped | `id-token`, `attestations: write` |
| `publish-pypi` | `attest-files` | yes: environment `pypi`, `pkg-python-dists`, trusted publishing (`skip-existing`, so a re-run after a partial upload completes it; PyPI's own attestations on) | skipped | PyPI (OIDC) |
| `publish-image` | `attest-files` | yes: loads `pkg-image-amd64` and `pkg-image-arm64`, pushes `:V-amd64` and `:V-arm64` and joins them as `:V` (`docker buildx imagetools create`); no `:latest` and no `:<major.minor>` yet | skipped | `packages: write` |
| `attest-image` | `publish-image` | yes: provenance for the `:V` manifest-list digest, `push-to-registry`, after its own ghcr login | skipped | `packages: write`, `id-token`, `attestations: write` |
| `github-release` | `publish-pypi`, `attest-image` | yes: a **draft** with every asset and the signature, the list compared with `release.py assets V`, the notes from `pkg-release-preview`; then published, and marked latest unless a prerelease | skipped | `contents: write` |
| `image-tags` | `github-release` | yes: `:<major.minor>` and (not for a prerelease) `:latest` pointed at `:V`'s digest | skipped | `packages: write` |
| `dry-run-summary` | `tests`, `package` | skipped | yes: the job summary: V, L, the asset list with sizes and hashes, the attestation subjects, the image tarballs loaded and their architectures, what each tag-only job would do and with which environment | nothing |

- **Order on a tag:** nothing becomes public until the signature passes its rules and both verifiers, and nothing irreversible happens until the files' provenance is made: a signing failure (the wrong key, a missing public key) or an attestation failure stops the release with PyPI, the registry and the release page untouched. Then PyPI and the image's version tags; then the image's provenance; then the GitHub release, published last and as a whole, so `releases/latest/download/<helper>` never points at a release that lacks a helper; then the moving image tags, so `:latest` never names an image whose release does not exist. `check_workflows.py` holds this `needs` graph and each job's permissions (rule 16), and RELEASING.md says which job to re-run after a failure at each point (P5.15).
- **PyPI's trusted publisher** names release.yml and `pypi`, so publishing stays in release.yml (a reusable workflow cannot publish under it).
- **Prereleases** (`-rc.N` and the like) publish everything but are never `latest` on GitHub or in the registry.
- **The notes** are the CHANGELOG section (P5.2) with the checksums and verification lines (P5.10).

---

## P5.12 Attestations

- **Made only for a tagged release**, in two jobs: `attest-files` (every release file and the signature) **before** anything is published, and `attest-image` (the `:V` manifest-list digest, pushed to the registry) after the image's push, with `packages: write` and its own ghcr login, by `actions/attest-build-provenance` (pinned) with the run's OIDC identity. Attestations of a public repository go to Sigstore's public transparency log, which is publishing: **a dry run makes none.** It proves the inputs instead: `assemble` computes the subject list (every file of `pkg-release-assets`), `dry-run-summary` shows it, and `check_workflows.py` holds each attest job's permissions and that its `subject-path` covers the folder the asset list is downloaded into (rule 16).
- **PyPI** adds its own attestations (PEP 740) through trusted publishing.
- **Verification** is documented (INSTALL.md, the release notes) as `gh attestation verify <file> --repo jprodgers/CITAR`, and Phase 6's deploy bundle runs it on the wheel before anything reaches the VPS (P5.13.5).

---

## P5.13 The deploy from the release wheel

### P5.13.1 The layout on the server

```
/opt/citar/releases/<pep440>/venv/            a venv per release, root-owned, read-only to the service
/opt/citar/releases/<pep440>/wheel, freeze.txt, SHA256SUMS line
/opt/citar/current -> releases/<pep440>       the release that runs (switched atomically)
/var/lib/citar/                               all state (citar:citar 0750); the units' working directory
/etc/citar/citar.env                          configuration and secrets (root:citar 0640), unchanged
/etc/citar/deploy-checks.conf                 the other sites' URLs, one a line (made on the box, never tracked)
/usr/local/sbin/citar-deploy                  the deploy script (installed by itself)
/usr/local/bin/citar-admin                    the admin wrapper
/var/log/citar-deploy.log                     one line a deploy: version, time, previous, backup
```

The units run console scripts from `/opt/citar/current/venv/bin` with `WorkingDirectory=/var/lib/citar`; a console script puts its own `bin/` folder, not the working directory, first on `sys.path`, and the job host starts with `python -P -m` (P5.6), so neither a source tree nor the service-writable state directory is ever on the import path (P2.9). `install.sh --server` and `citar setup --server` use the same layout.

### P5.13.2 The units and wrappers (`deploy/`)

- **`citar.service`:** `ExecStart=/opt/citar/current/venv/bin/citar serve` (settings read `CITAR_HOST` and `CITAR_PORT` from the env file, `citar/settings.py:265-266`), `WorkingDirectory=/var/lib/citar`, the sandbox as today, and the priorities 0.1.5's lab unit had and 0.1.6 lost (P5.0.9): **`CPUWeight=` and `IOWeight=`** in that unit's range (20 to 50; 5-10a starts at 50, and 5-11's measurement settles it, so the web server is not starved while the other sites still win the CPU under contention), `OOMScoreAdjust=500` as today. `MemoryMax`/`MemoryHigh` come from the staging measurement under one rule: **the server's measured peak plus the job host's memory figure stays under `MemoryHigh`** with 40 MB to spare (`MemoryHigh` throttles the whole cgroup, the web server included, so sizing against `MemoryMax` would throttle the server whenever a lab game ran); kept at 400M/320M unless the measurement needs more; the measured peaks and the rule are recorded beside them. The wizard renders this file's directives (a test holds the two equal), so there is one definition. Running the job host in a delegated child cgroup (`Delegate=`, its own `cpu.max`, `cpu.weight` 20 and `memory.max`) would make its CPU share hard; it is the fallback if 5-11's response-time gate fails, after checking how systemd 255 combines `Delegate=` with `ProtectControlGroups=yes` (which makes `/sys/fs/cgroup` read-only to the unit).
- **No `citar-lab` unit.** The lab's job host is a child of the server, inside its `MemoryMax`, under the host machine's CPU settings; Phase 6 sets those from the staging measurement after the fresh database exists (P5.15).
- **`citar-backup.sh`:** the database by `sqlite3 .backup` with its integrity check (as today), plus **the whole state directory** as a tarball without the live database files, the env file, and the release that wrote them (`readlink /opt/citar/current`); pruning after the new backup, as today.
- **`citar-admin.sh`** (tracked; installed as `/usr/local/bin/citar-admin`): `systemd-run --pty --wait --collect --quiet` as `citar` with the unit's `EnvironmentFile`, `WorkingDirectory` and file-system sandbox, so an admin command parses the env file exactly as the service does and can write nothing the service cannot. Like `citar-deploy`, it passes `--slice=$CITAR_DEPLOY_SLICE` to every `systemd-run` when that variable is set (staging sets it, P5.14; unset on the VPS).
- **`citar-secrets.sh`**, **`nginx-citar.conf`** unchanged in substance; the deploy never rewrites an installed nginx site (certbot edited it) and shows a difference instead.

### P5.13.3 `deploy/citar-deploy.sh`

One script, run as root on the box from an unpacked deploy bundle (P5.13.5) or from its installed copy. Every command has `--plan`, which prints each step it would take, with every path and command, and changes nothing (5-10b holds the plans to committed expected texts, as 4-14 held the service plans). When `CITAR_DEPLOY_SLICE` (or `--slice`) is set, every `systemd-run` it starts, scopes included, runs in that slice (staging sets it, P5.14; the VPS leaves it unset).

**The server migrates on every start and refuses a database newer than its scripts** (P5.0.9), so a rollback after a migration must restore the database. `install` therefore takes its snapshot with the service stopped, immediately before the switch, and restores it itself when a failed release has moved the revision.
- **`install --bundle DIR`** (an ordinary release):
  1. **Preflight:** root; the bundle's files against its `SHA256SUMS`; the wheel's name, version and platform tag (a version older than the running one is refused: going back is `rollback`'s); `python3.12` with `venv`; `sqlite3`; at least 1.5 GB free under `/opt` and `/var`; the running release (if any); every URL of `deploy-checks.conf` answers 200 **before** anything changes (a box already in trouble is not deployed to), and CITAR's own health.
  2. **Stage:** `releases/<P>/venv` made fresh (an existing one is refused without `--reinstall`); `pip install --no-cache-dir --only-binary=:all: --constraint <constraints> "<wheel>[server]"` inside `systemd-run --scope -p MemoryMax=500M -p CPUWeight=20` (in the slice when one is set) so the install cannot starve the other sites; `pip freeze` kept.
  3. **Verify the staged release** as `citar`, with the env file's non-state settings only and every state setting (`CITAR_DATA_DIR`, `CITAR_DB_URL` and the directories the env file names) pointed at a fresh temporary directory, removed afterwards, so the new code never opens the live database while the old release serves: `citar --version` is V; `citar._engine` loads from that venv without test operations; `citar doctor --quiet` passes, its job host verifiable (a FAIL line stops the deploy before anything has changed). A cutover verifies the same way.
  4. **Switch:** the live database's `alembic_version` read (`sqlite3`, read-only) and recorded; `systemctl stop citar`; `systemctl start citar-backup` (oneshot, so it waits; with the service stopped the snapshot is consistent and current), and the new backup must exist without `UNTRUSTWORTHY`; `current` replaced atomically (`ln -sfn` to a temporary name, `mv -T`); the units, `citar-admin`, `citar-backup`, `citar-secrets` and the script itself installed from the bundle into `/etc/systemd/system`, `/usr/local/bin` and `/usr/local/sbin` when they differ (the difference shown), so the box never keeps 0.1.5's backup, which skips most of the state directory; `daemon-reload`; `systemctl start citar`. The downtime (the backup and the start) is measured and recorded.
  5. **Health:** `/api/auth/config` 200 on 127.0.0.1 and through nginx (with the site's `Host`), and `/api/meta`'s version is V, within 60 s; a WebSocket handshake through nginx is answered by the app (an `Upgrade` the proxy passes). **On failure:** `systemctl stop citar`; if the database's revision differs from the one recorded in step 4, the database is restored from step 4's snapshot (the restore drill's path, P5.14); `current` goes back to the previous release; `systemctl start citar`; the health is checked again (the version on `/api/meta` is the previous one); and the script exits 1 naming what failed and whether the database was restored.
  6. **The other sites:** every URL again; a failure is reported loudly with the rollback command and exit 3, and nothing is done automatically (the cause may not be CITAR's; Phase 6 is attended).
  7. **Record** the deploy (version, time, previous release, revisions before and after, the backup's path); prune releases beyond three, never `current` or the one before it.
- **`cutover --bundle DIR`** (Phase 6's first deploy, from 0.1.5's layout): the preflight, which also recognises the 0.1.5 layout (`/opt/citar/citar/__init__.py`, `/opt/citar/.venv`, `citar-lab.service`); stop `citar` and `citar-lab`; 0.1.5's own backup; copy the installed unit files and scripts into the archive; disable and remove `citar-lab.service`; move `/opt/citar` to `/opt/citar-0.1.5`; move `/var/lib/citar` to `/var/lib/citar-archive-<stamp>` and make a fresh `/var/lib/citar` (the owner's "archive everything and start fresh"; the 0.1.6 server makes its database on first start); list the env file's keys that 0.1.6's `env.example` does not name (shown, not changed); then `install`'s steps 2-7, its switch without the snapshot (the archived state is the backup), and `uncutover` as its automatic rollback on any failure from the staging on; print the next steps (P5.15). A cutover's downtime includes the staging (the old tree must move before the new venvs exist, and a venv cannot be moved), a few minutes on the box; staging measures it.
- **`rollback [--to P] [--restore-backup DIR]`:** `current` to the previous (or named) release, restart, health. The database's revision is first checked against the target release's migrations (`citar/migrations` in its venv): a revision the target does not know is refused without `--restore-backup`, and the refusal names the backup the migrating deploy took (from the deploy log). With a backup, the service is stopped and the database and state restored from it first.
- **`uncutover`:** the cutover undone: the new layout and state moved aside, `/opt/citar-0.1.5` and the archived state put back, the saved units restored (`citar-lab` included), both started, health and the other sites checked.
- **`status`**, **`prune --keep N`**.

### P5.13.4 Limits on a 1 vCPU, 961 MB box

| Thing | How it is held |
|---|---|
| The server and its games | `citar.service`'s `MemoryMax`/`MemoryHigh` and `OOMScoreAdjust=500` (CITAR dies first, never the other sites' services); the job host's `RLIMIT_DATA` and `nice` inside it |
| CITAR against the other sites | `citar.service`'s `CPUWeight=`/`IOWeight=` (20 to 50, from 5-11's response-time measurement), so under contention the other sites' services win; the delegated child cgroup is the fallback (P5.13.2) |
| The lab | the host machine's CPU settings (server mode: one thread, 50%, the memory figure Phase 6 sets from the staging measurement so that the server's peak plus the host's figure stays under `MemoryHigh` with 40 MB to spare) |
| A deploy's pip install | a transient scope, `MemoryMax=500M`, `CPUWeight=20` |
| Releases on disk | three kept (a release's venv is about 200 MB) |

### P5.13.5 Where the wheel comes from

The laptop, never the box, fetches and checks it: `scripts/deploy/bundle.py V` downloads the release's linux-x64 wheel, `citar-P-server-constraints.txt` and `SHA256SUMS` (`gh release download vV`), checks the hashes, runs `gh attestation verify` on the wheel, adds `git archive vV deploy/ env.example`, and writes `citar-deploy-V.tar.gz` with its SHA-256 under `C:/dev/target`. `--from-dir DIR` builds a bundle from a dry run's artifacts or a local wheel (no attestation exists; it says so) for the staging run. Copying it to the box uses the operator's SSH details from the private log.

---

## P5.14 The staging dry run in WSL

**`scripts/deploy/staging.sh setup|run|cleanup`**, run as root inside WSL only (`wsl -d Ubuntu-24.04 -u root -- bash <copy in /root>`), on a copy of the branch and a deploy bundle, never under `/mnt/c`.

**Held to the VPS, not to WSL.** WSL has 12 CPUs, 7.8 GB, swappiness 60 and a faster core, and its `system.slice` delegates only memory and pids (P5.0.12), so a slice that merely names the VPS's figures would measure another machine. Staging therefore makes each difference explicit and proves each limit binds before it measures anything:
- **setup:** the apt packages a fresh VPS image lacks here (`python3-venv`, `sqlite3`, `nginx`), recorded.
  - **The slice:** `citar-staging.slice` with `CPUQuota=` scaled to the VPS's vCPU (`--cpu-quota`, default 60%: the review's estimate that a laptop core is about 1.6 times the VPS's; the figure used is recorded, and Phase 6's first measurement on the box corrects it for the next rehearsal), `MemoryMax=961M` and `MemorySwapMax=2G`; every staged unit joins it through a drop-in (`Slice=`), and `CITAR_DEPLOY_SLICE=citar-staging.slice` is exported, so `citar-deploy`'s pip scope and `citar-admin`'s transient units land in it too, and the deploy script runs unmodified.
  - **`vm.swappiness=10`**, the VPS's, for the run (the original value recorded; cleanup restores it). It is WSL's own kernel setting, inside WSL only.
  - **The other sites' load:** a **ballast** unit in the slice that allocates and touches the other sites' resident memory (`--ballast-mb N`, required, no default: the figure comes from the private log's measurement and lives in `ops/p5-notes.md`, never in a tracked file), so CITAR gets what it gets on the box, not the whole 961 MB; and **two stand-in sites** (an nginx server block, and a small Python HTTP unit in the slice that does a fixed slice of CPU work per request, like php-fpm), listed in `/etc/citar/deploy-checks.conf`, with a load generator calling the Python one at a steady rate during the game and lab steps and recording its response times.
  - **Probes, before anything is measured:** the slice's `memory.max`, `memory.swap.max` and `cpu.max` read back equal to what was asked; a transient probe unit in the slice with a small `MemoryMax` and no swap allocates past it and must be OOM-killed, with `oom_kill` rising in the slice's `memory.events`; a busy loop in the slice for five seconds must use the quota's share of a CPU (`cpu.stat`'s `usage_usec` within 10% of quota times wall time). A limit that does not bind fails setup. `IOWeight=` and `AllowedCPUs=` cannot be proven here (no `io` or `cpuset` controller in WSL): VPS-only, named so in the as-built note.
  - **A 0.1.5 deployment** as the VPS has it: `git archive v0.1.5` into `/opt/citar`, its `.venv` with its `requirements.txt`, its `citar.service` and `citar-lab.service` (CITAR on `127.0.0.1` only), started once to make its state, and an nginx site for CITAR on a loopback port with the `connection_upgrade` map.
- **run:** `citar-deploy cutover` with the bundle (a local wheel in 5-11, **the dry run's CI-built wheel** in 5-13); then, through `scripts/deploy/staging_check.py` (standard library only):
  - the first administrator made with `citar-admin`; **the host machine's settings set through the admin API** (`PUT /api/machines/{sid}/settings`) to `host_cpu_defaults(mode="server", cpus=1, ram_mb=961)`, as the VPS computes them, and the job host's `ready` frame must report about 240 MB (WSL's own defaults would give it 1,024 MB of `RLIMIT_DATA`, and under `MemoryMax` that turns a clean allocation failure into a cgroup OOM kill);
  - a 4-bot small game played for 40 rounds through the API; a lab experiment of three duel games submitted as `citar` and played by the job host under the unit's sandbox;
  - the measurements: `MemoryPeak` and `MemoryCurrent` of `citar.service` idle, with the game and with the lab; the job host's peak from its frames; the slice's `memory.events` `oom_kill`, which must stay 0; seconds per round; the pip install's time and peak inside the slice; the switch's downtime; **the loaded stand-in's response times** idle and with a lab game running, whose p95 must stay within twice the idle p95 plus 50 ms (the bound 5-11 records with the `CPUWeight=` it settles on);
  - **an upgrade that migrates:** `citar-deploy install` of `-rc.1`, a wheel built locally in the maturin container from a scratch clone bumped past the cut-over version that also adds an empty migration; then `rollback`, refused without `--restore-backup` (the revision moved) and accepted with the backup `install` took; then `install` of `-rc.1` again;
  - **a failed upgrade that migrated:** `-rc.2`, a planted release that adds a second empty migration and whose server exits after migrating, must come back by itself, healthy on `-rc.1` at `-rc.1`'s revision, with `install` exiting 1 and saying the database was restored;
  - `citar-backup` and a restore drill (the snapshot opened, integrity-checked, and a second CITAR started against a copy on another port, reading its accounts); `uncutover`, with 0.1.5 answering again.
  - The stand-in sites answer 200 before and after every step, and the CITAR site's WebSocket upgrade passes nginx.
- **cleanup:** units, drop-ins, the slice, the ballast, the load generator, the `citar` user, `/opt/citar*`, `/var/lib/citar*`, `/etc/citar`, the nginx sites and the stand-ins removed; `vm.swappiness` restored; nginx stopped and disabled (the packages stay, recorded); S10's WSL items checked. The logs and measurements are copied under the package's target folder (`C:/dev/target/citar-p-5-xx/staging/`), and the figures that matter go into the as-built note.

Phase 6 runs it again with the published wheel before touching the VPS (P5.15).

---

## P5.15 The release checklist Phase 6 follows

`packaging/RELEASING.md` (5-09; walked through in dry-run form by 5-13) holds this list, each step a command:

1. **Before:** Phase 6's soak, QA and security review done; the owner's go-ahead. The version number decided (owner question). If updates are to work in this release: the owner has made `helper-signing` (required reviewer, the secret) and merged the public key into `update-keys.pub` (WORKERS.md); `gh api repos/jprodgers/CITAR/environments` shows it.
2. **Bump** on a branch from `main` (after `v0.1.6` merged): `python scripts/release.py bump 0.1.6 --date <today>`; review the diff; `release.py check --version 0.1.6`; `scripts/check_links.py --strict`; a pull request; every workflow green on it.
3. **Dry run** of that branch: `gh workflow run release.yml --ref <branch> -f version=0.1.6` (or `release-dry-run.yml`, if P5.11's fallback was taken), watched to green.
4. **Merge** to `main`; `main`'s CI green.
5. **Tag** the merge commit: `git tag -a v0.1.6 -m "CITAR 0.1.6"`, `git push origin v0.1.6`.
6. **Watch** release.yml; approve `sign-helper-manifest` when it waits for its reviewer. **If a tag-only job fails:** before `attest-files` passes, nothing is public: fix, delete the tag (`git push origin :refs/tags/v0.1.6`, the owner's step) and tag again. From then on re-run the failed job and those after it (`gh run rerun <id> --failed`): `publish-pypi` completes with `skip-existing`; `publish-image` and `attest-image` push the same saved images; `github-release` finds its draft and finishes it; `image-tags` moves the two tags last. RELEASING.md lists each job's state after a failure and the re-run for it.
7. **Verify:** `python scripts/release_checksums.py 0.1.6 --verify` (every asset against `SHA256SUMS`, PyPI's hashes against the built files; rehearsed by 5-13 in its `--base-url`/`--pypi-json` form); `gh attestation verify` on a wheel and a helper; `pip install citar==0.1.6` in a fresh venv takes a wheel, not the sdist; the image answers its health route, checked with **`podman run` in WSL** (the laptop has no Docker; the command is RELEASING.md's); a helper from `releases/latest/download/` reports 0.1.6 (run with `--home` in a temporary folder).
8. **Package managers:** the `pkg-release-preview` artifact's manifests (or `release_checksums.py 0.1.6 --write`); `scripts/release/managers.sh 0.1.6` clones the tap and the bucket into a temporary folder, copies the files (the tap: `Formula/citar-helper.rb` added, `Formula/citar.rb` removed; the bucket: `citar.json`, `citar-helper.json`), shows the diff, and pushes only with `--push`; `wingetcreate submit` for `JimmieRodgers.CITAR` and `JimmieRodgers.CITAR.Helper` (`winget validate` first).
9. **Deploy** (with the owner's go-ahead): `scripts/deploy/bundle.py 0.1.6`; the staging rehearsal with that bundle (P5.14, with `--ballast-mb` from the private log and the recorded `--cpu-quota`); copy to the box; `/etc/citar/deploy-checks.conf` made from the private log; `sudo bash deploy/citar-deploy.sh cutover --bundle <dir> --plan`, read, then without `--plan`. Rollback: `sudo citar-deploy uncutover`.
10. **After:** the first administrator (`sudo citar-admin create-user ...`); the host's machine settings from the staging measurement; one lab game's seconds per round on the box compared with staging's, and the ratio recorded as the next rehearsal's `--cpu-quota`; helpers re-registered with the new helper; `ops/DEPLOY_LOG.md` written.

---

## P5.16 The docs pass

Each file is read whole against the code at 5-12's base; every statement about installing, building, releasing, deploying or the 0.1.5 engine and worker is checked; what changes:

| File | What changes |
|---|---|
| `README.md` | Install: wheels for five platforms, the installer, `install.sh`, pipx, Docker (multi-arch), the helper's packages; "building from source needs Rust"; release verification in one line |
| `DESIGN.md` | Read for stale statements; a line on packaging pointing at ARCHITECTURE |
| `CONTRIBUTING.md` | "Releasing" points at `packaging/RELEASING.md` and `release.py`; package.yml beside test.yml; `check_workflows.py` and its rules (actions pinned by SHA and moved by Dependabot, the `pkg-` artifact names, nothing installed where a secret is held); the smoke test; building wheels locally (maturin, the manylinux container in WSL); `requirements.txt` gone |
| `SECURITY.md` | The release chain: actions pinned by SHA, attestations (files before anything is published), `SHA256SUMS`, the update key's custody and its rules (P5.4.2: a job that builds and installs nothing, a dry run that never names its environment), the packaged helper; `/api/meta` no longer reveals paths; supported versions |
| `NOTICE.md` | Third-party notices (P5.5) |
| `CHANGELOG.md` | **Compatibility** gains "Installing and packaging" (below); Added and Fixed for Phase 5 (the wheels, the image, the helper packages, the deploy, P5.0.11's defects) |
| `KNOWN_ISSUES.md` | "Platforms and packaging" rewritten: wheel and helper platforms and their one glibc floor (2.28), no musl/32-bit/Windows-ARM64/free-threaded wheels, unsigned and unnotarised binaries, winget pending at Microsoft, package managers lag the release by the manifest step; "Server": one game a core, the lab inside `MemoryMax`, local backups |
| `docs/INSTALL.md` | Every route for 0.1.6: the installer, `install.sh`/`install.ps1` (wheels only, the platform table, `--worker`), pipx, Homebrew (helper only), Scoop and winget (game and helper), Docker, from source; verifying downloads; removing each |
| `docs/QUICKSTART.md`, `FAQ.md`, `TROUBLESHOOTING.md` | Install lines; "no wheel for my platform"; SmartScreen and quarantine; a helper from a package manager not updating itself |
| `docs/PLAYING.md`, `AI_PLAYERS.md` | The MCP command from `/api/meta` (`citar-mcp`, the bundle's `citar.exe mcp`) |
| `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` | A "Packaging and releases" section: the build matrix, package.yml and release.yml, the label, the server's release layout |
| `docs/API.md` | `/api/meta`'s new fields |
| `docs/CONFIGURATION.md` | `CITAR_BUILD_ID` (build time), `CITAR_RELEASE_URL` (install scripts) |
| `docs/BENCHMARKS.md`, `BOTS.md`, `research/BOT_TUNING.md`, `REPORTS.md`, `SCENARIOS.md`, `MODDING.md`, `reference.md` | Read; install and lab-runner statements corrected (the lab inside the server; modding from a wheel install with `CITAR_RULESET_DIR`); `reference.md`'s modules exist |
| `docs/server/DEPLOY.md` | The three routes on the wheel; the release layout; `citar-deploy`; the units (their CPU and IO weights); limits on a small box (5-10a/5-10b left these to 5-12) |
| `docs/server/RUNBOOK.md` | "Deploying a new version" and rollback with `citar-deploy` (a rollback across a migration needs the deploy's backup); restores from the new backup; the staging rehearsal (5-11 wrote its section); the five commands' paths |
| `docs/server/VPS.md` | Capacity with the lab inside the server (the `MemoryHigh` rule, the weights, staging's figures); swap; disk for three releases |
| `docs/server/WORKERS.md` | The helper's packages and `--worker`; "Signing helper updates" with P5.4.2's rules and the dry run |
| `docs/server/ACCOUNTS.md`, `OAUTH.md` | Read; `python -m citar.server` replaced by `citar serve` where a command is shown |
| `packaging/README.md` | Rewritten for 0.1.6: every route, what CI builds, what Phase 6 copies where |
| `wiki/` | Regenerated by `scripts/build_wiki.py` and committed |

**The CHANGELOG's "Installing and packaging" compatibility item** says, for someone coming from 0.1.5: CITAR installs from wheels for Windows x64, macOS 11+ (Apple silicon and Intel) and Linux x64 and arm64 (glibc 2.28+), Python 3.11 or newer; elsewhere `pip install citar` builds from the sdist with a Rust toolchain; `install.sh` and `install.ps1` install wheels only and `--worker`/`-Worker` install the helper; the Homebrew tap now ships only the helper (`brew uninstall citar`, then `install.sh` or pipx for the game, `brew install jprodgers/citar/citar-helper` for the helper); Scoop and winget gain a helper package; the Docker image is multi-arch and built from the wheels; the Windows installer upgrades in place (same AppId and data folder); release assets add the wheels, the packaged helper archives, `SHA256SUMS`, `THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.txt` and attestations, and the helper's download names are unchanged (its Linux builds run on the game's floor, glibc 2.28); a self-hosted server deploys the wheel into the release layout, `citar-lab` and `requirements.txt` are gone, the lab runs inside `citar.service`, which now carries the lab's lower CPU and IO weights, the backup covers the whole state directory, and a rollback across a migration restores the deploy's backup; release builds report their label (`v0.1.6`) in `citar doctor` and `citar-helper --version`.

**Checks:** `check_links.py --strict`, `mkdocs build --strict`, the wiki committed, and a stale-terms scan (`scripts/ci/stale_terms.py`, 5-12, run by test.yml's lint job) that refuses, outside the CHANGELOG's history and the Compatibility notes, the phrases of the old routes (the `git archive` deploy, `requirements.txt` installs, the game's Homebrew formula, `citar-lab`, `python -m citar.server` in a unit, the PyPI sdist in a manifest).

## P5.17 CI changes

| Workflow | Change |
|---|---|
| `package.yml` (new, 5-02b) | P5.3-P5.10: the wheels, the sdist and its build, the helpers and the manifest, the bundle and installer, the images, the install scripts, `assemble`, the package managers; `pull_request`, `push: main`, `workflow_dispatch`, `workflow_call`; every artifact `pkg-...` |
| `test.yml` | `workflow_call` with `mode`/`version`/`date`; `release.py prepare` first in every job; concurrency `tests-${{ github.workflow }}-${{ github.ref }}`; the Linux suite on **3.11, 3.12, 3.13 and 3.14** (the classifiers claim 3.14); `package`, `installer` and `docker` moved to package.yml; lint runs `release.py check`, `check_workflows.py` and (5-12) `stale_terms.py`; the `requirements.txt` step goes with the file (5-10a); its artifact names unchanged (`ext-<os>`, `helper-<os>`) |
| `release.yml` | 5-02a reduces it to its gate (one job that fails with the Phase 5 notice; no build, no publishing job, no environment) until 5-09 writes P5.11; the old jobs' work moves to package.yml (5-02b to 5-08) and the new tag-only jobs (5-09) |
| `rust.yml` | Its path filters gain `package.yml` (xtask reads it); lint runs `cargo xtask licenses --check` after `cargo fetch --locked` |
| `helper.yml` | Its `artifact` matrix stays equal to package.yml's `helpers` (the names test); its Linux builds move into the manylinux_2_28 containers with the glibc check (P5.4.1) |
| every workflow | Every remote `uses:` pinned to a commit SHA with its version in a comment (5-02a); Dependabot's existing weekly `github-actions` group moves the pins |
| `determinism.yml`, `nightly.yml`, `docs.yml` | The pins only; never dispatched by Phase 5 |

**`scripts/ci/check_workflows.py`** (5-02a, extended in 5-03 and 5-09; PyYAML, which the dev extra brings through mkdocs and the lint job installs) proves, before any dispatch and on every pull request:
1. every workflow parses (PyYAML reads the `on` key as `True`; the script knows) and sets `permissions: contents: read` at the top;
2. a job **publishes** if it names an environment, asks for `id-token`, `pages`, `packages`, `contents`, `attestations`, `deployments` or `pull-requests` write, reads a secret other than `GITHUB_TOKEN`, uses a publishing action (`pypa/gh-action-pypi-publish`, `softprops/action-gh-release`, `actions/attest-build-provenance`, `actions/deploy-pages`, `docker/login-action`) or runs a publishing command (`gh release`, `twine upload`, `docker push`, `imagetools create`, `git push`, a mutating `gh api`); every publishing job is either in release.yml, carrying the tag guard as a conjunct of its job-level `if:`, or in docs.yml, carrying 2-06b's guard to `refs/heads/main` (the site and the wiki, published from main only) either on the job or, as the `wiki` job has it, on exactly the steps that read a secret or push (so a branch run still checks that the pages generate); neither guard may be weakened by `||`, `always()`, `!cancelled()` or `failure()`, and no other workflow publishes;
3. package.yml and test.yml publish nothing and name no environment and no secret;
4. a called workflow's concurrency group contains `github.workflow` and differs from its caller's;
5. no `run:` block interpolates `inputs.*`, `github.event.inputs.*` or `github.head_ref` (they reach shells through `env:`);
6. every maturin-action step pins `maturin-version: ${{ env.MATURIN_VERSION }}`;
7. every job of package.yml and test.yml runs `release.py prepare` after the checkout and before its first build or test step;
8. release.yml has no build step (cargo, maturin, PyInstaller, ISCC, `docker build`);
9. no upload path can include a `*.key` file or the throwaway key folder;
10. release.yml calls neither nightly.yml nor docs.yml, and no workflow runs `build_wiki.py --push`;
11. **every remote `uses:` in every workflow is pinned** to a 40-character commit SHA with a version comment (`@<sha> # v7.0.1`); local `./` references are exempt; **every image** a workflow builds or runs in is pinned by a `sha256` digest (a `docker://` step, a job's `container:` and `services:`, and maturin-action's `container:` on Linux, each matrix leg's), and a maturin-action step whose `manylinux` asks for a container without naming one (it would take the action's own image, by a moving tag) fails (added in 5-02b's fix round); an image a script runs (`docker run "$IMAGE"`, whose words no check can read) is written where this rule reads it, a matrix leg's `container` or `image` or a matrix or `env:` key ending in either (`FEDORA_IMAGE`), and needs its digest too (added in 5-03);
12. **a job that calls a workflow** (`uses: ./.github/workflows/...`) passes no `secrets:` (never `inherit`) and sets `permissions: contents: read`;
13. **artifact names:** package.yml uploads only `pkg-...`, release.yml only `rel-...`, no other workflow either prefix; the upload names, matrix-expanded, are unique across test.yml, package.yml and release.yml; every download in package.yml and release.yml names one artifact exactly or the pattern `pkg-*` or `rel-*` (never no name, never another pattern);
14. **environments:** release.yml names `pypi` (its `publish-pypi` only) or the tag expression of P5.11, docs.yml names `github-pages` (its `deploy` only), and no other environment or workflow appears; a literal `environment: helper-signing` fails;
15. **nothing installs or builds where power is held:** a step that reads a secret other than `GITHUB_TOKEN` runs no package tool (cargo, pip, pipx, npm, npx, uv, maturin) and is not a `uses:` step; in release.yml, a job that reads such a secret or holds `id-token: write` or any write permission runs no package tool or `docker build`, its `uses:` are only on an allow-list (`actions/checkout` with `persist-credentials: false`, `actions/download-artifact`, `actions/upload-artifact` and the publishing actions of rule 2), and no `setup-*` action;
16. **release.yml's order and permissions** (5-09): the `needs` graph and each tag-only job's permissions are P5.11's table; `publish-pypi` and `publish-image` need `attest-files`, which needs `check-signature`; `image-tags` needs `github-release`, which creates a draft first; `attest-files`' `subject-path` covers the folder `pkg-release-assets` is downloaded into and the signature.

`tests/test_workflows.py` runs it on the real files and on a planted fixture for each rule, each of which must fail naming its job and rule.

CONTRIBUTING names both scripts and `pin --write`; the wiki is regenerated (never pushed).
- **Decided beyond the text.**
  - **What publishes** (rule 2) is wider than the design's list:
    - any `write` permission, or `write-all`;
    - `actions/attest`, and `docker/build-push-action` with `push`;
    - gh's other writes: `workflow run`, and changes to `pr`, `issue`, `secret`, `variable`, `label`, `repo`, `run` and `cache` (`gh release view`, `list` and `download` are reads);
    - `docker build --push`, `podman push`, `npm`/`cargo`/`uv`/`poetry publish` and `maturin publish|upload`.
    A command is found past its global options (`git -C dir push`, `gh -R o/r release create`).
  - **A guard** is a conjunct of the condition's top-level `&&` chain, with parentheses flattened:
    - `(guard) || x` fails, and so does a guard inside a `!`;
    - `always()`, `cancelled()` and `failure()` fail anywhere in the condition; `success()` and further conjuncts are allowed;
    - the `startsWith` literal must begin `refs/tags/v`;
    - an `if:` with text around its `${{ }}`, or one the reader cannot parse, fails.
    The tag expression of rule 14 is read the same way: `(guards && '<name>') || ''`, with the name last.
  - **The other rules, beyond their wording:**
    - Rule 3 counts `GITHUB_TOKEN` too ("no secret"), whether written `secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN` or `github.token`, and refuses a `workflow_call` that declares secrets.
    - Rule 4 also checks a called workflow's job-level groups. "Differs from its caller's" is judged as the caller runs it: `github.workflow` is read as the caller's `name`. A workflow-level group has no job, so that finding names the file and the rule.
    - Rule 5 also refuses the pull request's title, body, head ref and label, and the head commit's message and author.
    - Rule 6 also requires the workflow or the job to define `MATURIN_VERSION`.
    - Rule 7: only the checkout, `actions/setup-*`, `docker/setup-*`, the caches, `taiki-e/install-action` and `download-artifact` may come between the checkout and prepare. Prepare carries no `if:` and no `continue-on-error`.
    - Rule 8 adds `python -m build`, `pip wheel`, `buildx build`, maturin-action and build-push-action.
    - Rule 9 reads "can include" as any of: a last path part that matches a key's name (`*`, `*.key`, `update-signing.key`); a `throwaway` folder; the runner's temporary folder whole; a path with `..`; the runner's own folders. So rule 9 already covers 5-03's key folder, and 5-03 only keeps its uploads outside it.
    - Rule 10 adds `gh workflow run nightly|docs` in release.yml.
    - Rule 11 reads the `uses:` lines as text, since YAML drops comments. It refuses a `uses:` written where its line cannot be read (a flow mapping), and a `docker://` image without a `sha256` digest.
    - Rule 13 expands each matrix as GitHub does (the product, `exclude`, then `include`) and fills in `matrix.*`, `runner.os` (from `runs-on`) and `strategy.job-index`. It counts `upload-pages-artifact` (default name `github-pages`) for the prefixes, and an unnamed upload is `artifact`.
    - Rule 14 accepts the tag expression only for `helper-signing` (`TAG_ENVIRONMENTS`), the one name P5.11 writes that way and P5.18.2 allows besides `github-pages` and `pypi`. A misspelt name would otherwise pass and create a new environment, with no reviewer and no secret, at Phase 6's tag push.
    - Rules 8 and 15 refuse a run step in a shell the check cannot read: anything but bash, sh, pwsh, powershell and cmd (`shell: python`, a `perl {0}` template, an expression), taken from the step's `shell:` or else the job's or workflow's `defaults.run.shell`. That is a rule 8 finding anywhere in release.yml, and a rule 15 finding in a step reading a secret or in a powered release.yml job.
  - **Shell is read word by word.** Quotes are removed, comments dropped, and commands split at `;`, `&`, `&&`, `|`, `||`, `(`, `)`, backticks and newlines, each remembering the operator that ended it; a `${{ }}` is a placeholder word. Redirections (`2>&1`, `&>log`) stay with their command, and launchers are seen through (`sudo` with its options, `env`, `timeout`, `nice`); `pip3`, `pip3.12` and `pip-3.12` are pip. A heredoc's body is data (`cat > f <<EOF`, `python3 - <<PY`), except when a shell reads it as its script: `bash <<EOF`, `bash -s <<'EOF'`, or `cat <<EOF | bash` through a pipeline (not `bash x.sh <<EOF`, whose stdin is data). Also read as scripts: `bash -c`/`-lc`, `eval`, a here-string given to a shell (`bash <<< '...'`), and the words after `pwsh -Command` (or `-c`) and `cmd /c`. A `<<` inside quotes or a comment starts no heredoc. So docs.yml's notice, `echo "... 'python scripts/build_wiki.py --push' ..."`, is text and not a push. A script written to a file and run later is not read; the permission and secret rules are the barrier there.
  - **Rule 5 covers every workflow** (its text says "no `run:` block"). So rust.yml's chaos step and nightly.yml's four chaos and soak steps, which wrote `${{ inputs.* || default }}` into their scripts, now pass each input through the step's `env:`. The defaults and commands are unchanged. P5.17's table says nightly.yml takes "the pins only"; this is the one exception.
  - **S1c:** the package's venv is Windows' Python 3.14.6, as every Phase 3 to 5 venv has been. So S1 is the 3.14 run, and no second venv was made.

  - **Heredocs a shell reads.** The shell reader dropped every heredoc body, so `bash <<'EOF'` or `bash -s <<EOF` hid its commands from rules 2, 3, 8 and 15. A heredoc's body is now read when a shell runs it.

## P5.18 Cross-cutting rules

### P5.18.1 Safety on this laptop (binding on every package)

P4.7.1, unchanged, with Phase 5's additions:
1. **Never** install a system service, a scheduled task or a startup entry on Windows, change a system or security setting (firewall, Defender, UAC, any registry value), or elevate to administrator. **Never install anything on Windows:** pip only into the package's own venv under `C:/dev/venv`; no Inno Setup, Docker, Podman, Scoop, winget package or Homebrew on Windows (those run on CI runners only; WSL's apt installs are rule 8's).
2. **Never** touch the owner's real CITAR data or configuration: every server, CLI, QA, smoke, bundle or install-script run uses its own data directory, database and configuration (`CITAR_DATA_DIR` and the state variables under the package's target folder), never `%LOCALAPPDATA%\CITAR`, the main checkout's `config/`, port 8799 or `C:/dev/citar-play`. Every helper run uses an isolated `--home`; a release or packaged helper (which has no development guard, so its home would default to the real `%LOCALAPPDATA%\CITAR`) runs on the laptop only through the gate harness `scripts/ci/gate_run.py helper` (5-03), which refuses a run without `--home` under the package's target folder and sets `CITAR_DATA_DIR` there too; S10 records `helper.json` and the machine key around every such gate.
3. **Installer and install-script tests** run into temporary prefixes and never install into the owner's real Python, PATH, Start menu or registry: `install.ps1` only with `-Prefix`, `-BinDir` under the package's target folder and `-NoPath`, through `gate_run.py install-ps1` (5-07); `install.sh` only with `HOME`, `--prefix` and `--bin-dir` in a temporary folder and `--no-profile`; the Inno installer is checked by a generated-script test locally and run only on a CI runner.
4. **Never** generate a release signing private key into the repository or commit any private key; `crates/citar-jobs/update-keys.pub` stays empty until the owner commits the public key; throwaway keys live in temporary folders outside any git working tree (`keygen` refuses otherwise); S12 scans.
5. **Never** connect a test helper to `citar.jimmieprodgers.com` or any non-local server; downloads in tests come from loopback servers (`CITAR_RELEASE_URL`).
6. **Never** load, unload or swap an LM Studio model or change its settings (Phase 5 has no real-model gate).
7. **Never touch the VPS:** no SSH, no copy, no deploy. The deploy is Phase 6's, with the owner's go-ahead.
8. **WSL** is `wsl -d Ubuntu-24.04 -u claude`; root (`-u root`) only inside WSL, where a gate needs it (apt installs, the staging run), each recorded in `ops/p5-notes.md`; never the WSL password; builds in `~/` from a `git bundle` or clone, never under `/mnt/c`; **a WSL script never acts on the folder it starts in** (wsl.exe starts in Windows' current folder, for these sessions the main checkout: 5-04's incident, P5.5's as-built note): `wsl -d Ubuntu-24.04 -u claude --cd /home/claude`, the script copied to WSL's own disk and run from there, `cd "$HOME"` its first command, then before any command that writes, `pwd -P` and `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` checked equal to its clone (stop otherwise), and every checkout named explicitly (`release.py ... --root "$CLONE"`, `git -C "$CLONE"`); no quote inside a `${VAR:?message}` message (it opens a string that swallows the script's next lines); `wsl ... -- bash -c '...$var...'` expands `$var` in an outer shell first, so such commands go in a script file; a server inside WSL binds `0.0.0.0` only after `wslinfo --networking-mode` says `nat`, else `127.0.0.1`; the staging run's system changes are removed by its `cleanup`, whose checks are S10's.
9. **The main checkout** is only read by implementers and reviewers; merge agents merge and push from it as in Phase 4, never staging, committing or reverting the owner's `.claude/launch.json`. Worktree agents never push.
10. **Never** push a tag, create a release, publish to PyPI or ghcr, push to `jprodgers/homebrew-citar` or `jprodgers/scoop-citar`, submit to winget, publish the wiki (`build_wiki.py --push`, which uses this machine's own git credentials), create an environment, a secret or a repository setting, add the `nightly` label to PR #5, or dispatch nightly.yml or docs.yml.
11. **Every server on Windows binds loopback explicitly:** server mode's default host is `0.0.0.0` (`citar/settings.py:265`), and a new, unsigned executable listening on all interfaces raises Windows Defender Firewall's prompt, whose dismissal adds block rules (a firewall change, rule 1). So every server run on Windows, server mode included, sets `CITAR_HOST=127.0.0.1` and a free port other than 8799, through `scripts/ci/gate_run.py server` (5-03, used from 5-05), which refuses to start one without them. S10 records the firewall rules naming `citar` or `python` (`Get-NetFirewallRule`, read only) before and after.

### P5.18.2 Dispatch rules

- **Who may dispatch release.yml:** the merge agent of 5-09 after its push (the first dry run); the merge agent of any later package that changed release.yml, package.yml or test.yml, after its push; and 5-13 (its implementation agent on the pushed head; its merge agent again after pushing any fix). Nobody else. test.yml's own `workflow_dispatch` may be used by the same agents when PR #5's run for the same head is missing or was cancelled.
- **Only** `gh workflow run release.yml --ref v0.1.6 -f version=0.1.6` (the owner's version number if they choose another; `release-dry-run.yml` instead if P5.11's fallback is taken), on the **pushed** `v0.1.6`; never a tag ref, never another branch.
- **Before every dispatch:** S8 at the pushed head (`check_workflows.py` green: no publishing job can run outside a tag push, and no environment but `pypi` is named outside the tag expression); `gh api repos/jprodgers/CITAR/environments --jq '[.environments[].name]'` is exactly `["github-pages","pypi"]`, or `["github-pages","helper-signing","pypi"]` once the owner has made `helper-signing` (the owner's answer of 2026-10-19: the key, the environment and its secret may be set up at any time; any other environment stops the phase); `git ls-remote --tags origin` and `gh release list` recorded.
- **Watched to the end:** `gh run watch <id> --exit-status`; afterwards the same three readings, unchanged (a new environment, tag or release stops everything and is reported to the owner).
- **Bounded:** at most three fix-and-push rounds per dispatching agent, each fix through the local gates first; after the third failure the agent stops and reports the failing job's log.
- **package.yml at a merge push** runs by itself (PR #5's `pull_request` event): the merge agents of 5-02a to 5-12 watch that run and test.yml's for their push (`gh run list --workflow package.yml --branch v0.1.6 --event pull_request`), with the same three-round bound. That is not a dispatch.

### P5.18.3 Disk

- **Every package builds its own inputs from its own head**, under `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=C:/dev/target/citar-p-5-xx`: no gate uses a wheel, helper or bundle from an earlier package (each merge deletes them, and a later package's Python changes, 5-05's among them, would make them wrong anyway). maturin's `--out`, PyInstaller's `--distpath`/`--workpath`, sdist builds, scratch clones (5-01's, 5-11's and 5-13's bumped ones), deploy bundles, staging logs (`<target>/staging/`) and podman build contexts are all under that target folder. **Nothing builds into the OneDrive checkout** (no `target/`, `dist/`, `build/` or `installer/output/` there; `git status --ignored` checked at the end).
- **Venvs** are `C:/dev/venv/p-5-xx` and, when a gate needs another (the smoke's 3.11 and 3.14, S1c's 3.14, the bundle's 3.12, a bumped clone's), `C:/dev/venv/p-5-xx-<purpose>`; pip installs only into them.
- **The merge step deletes** `C:/dev/target/citar-p-5-xx` and every `C:/dev/venv/p-5-xx*` after the push, and lists `C:/dev/target` and `C:/dev/venv` to show nothing of the package remains; reviewers reuse the package's own target folder and venvs and never make others.
- **WSL:** one clone per package (`~/p5/p-5-xx`, removed at its merge); one shared cargo target `~/target/citar-p5`, kept between packages for its cache; podman's images and containers removed at each package's end except the base images (`python:3.12-slim-trixie`, the manylinux and maturin containers). **5-13 removes all of it**: `~/target/citar-p5`, `~/p5`, and podman's storage (`podman system reset`), recording `du -sh` of each and `df` of WSL's root before and after. The Phase 4 WSL targets (`~/target/citar-p-4-*`), the old `C:/dev/venv/p-2-*` to `p-4-*` venvs and the stray `target/` in the main checkout are the orchestrator's or owner's to remove; packages leave them alone.

### P5.18.4 CI parity and CI-only gates

- **Locally:** Windows x64 natively (the Windows wheel, bundle, helper, `install.ps1`), Linux x64 in WSL (the manylinux_2_28 x86_64 wheel and helper through the containers under podman, the image under podman, `install.sh`, the deploy and the staging), Python 3.11, 3.12 and 3.14 on Windows, 3.12 in WSL, `--locked` everywhere.
- **CI-only, named in each package:** macOS arm64 and x64, Linux arm64, Inno Setup's compile and the silent install, Scoop, winget, Homebrew, Docker's buildx and both native images, the attestation and publishing steps (tag-only, never run in Phase 5).
- **What only a release run exercises, and how each is covered earlier:**
  - *The version bump.* A dry run bumps every runner's checkout (P5.2), so the whole suite first runs bumped there. 5-01 rehearses it locally first (the whole S1 suite, with `cargo xtask develop`'s helpers and the update end-to-end tests, and S7's nextest in a bumped clone), and 5-13 repeats it at the exit.
  - *The shared artifact namespace.* Only a release run holds test.yml's and package.yml's artifacts together; rule 13 (the `pkg-`/`rel-` prefixes, named downloads, unique names) makes a collision a check failure on every pull request.
  - *The calling jobs.* `secrets:` and permissions on the two `uses:` jobs are rule 12's.
  - *The tag-only jobs* are the only release-only code: rules 2, 11 and 14 to 16 and the dry run's summary prove them, and none runs before Phase 6.
  Everything else in a release run is package.yml's and test.yml's, which run on every pull request.

### P5.18.5 The standard gates

Every package's gates include, besides its own (P4.7.5's, renumbered where Phase 5 adds):
- **S1** the Python suite (`python -m unittest discover -s tests`) in the package's venv, with `cargo xtask develop` when Rust changed; its count recorded. **S1b** the suite on Python 3.12 under `TZ=UTC0` (`C:/dev/venv/py312-check` with `CITAR_EXT_DIR` at the package's extension). From 5-02a, **S1c** the suite on Windows' Python 3.14 (`C:/dev/venv/p-5-xx-py314`) when the package changes Python.
- **S2** ruff (and `sync_requirements.py --check` until 5-10a retires it). **S3** `audit_routes.py --strict`. **S4** `check_links.py --strict`. **S5** when `docs/` changed: `mkdocs build --strict` and `build_wiki.py` (never `--push`) with `wiki/` committed. **S6** the front-end checks.
- **S7** when Rust (xtask included) changed: fmt, clippy `-D warnings`, nextest, the doctests, `cargo xtask check`, the refcheck run, `cargo golden check`, the `jobs` set in `release`.
- **S8** when a workflow or a script one runs changed: every workflow parses; `scripts/ci/check_workflows.py` (from 5-02a); `cargo xtask check`; `node --test citar/web/tests/helper.test.mjs`.
- **S9** the package's Linux half in WSL, as its gates name it (Rust in WSL when citar-helper or citar-jobs changed).
- **S10** the owner's data and the machine are as they were, before and after: P4.7.5's list (the two `servers.json` hashes, `citar.db` and `secret_key` mtimes, `helper.json`, machine keys, `config/worker.json`, no helper, job-host or server process, no `citar-helper` service, Run value or task, no `CITAR_*` variable, the main checkout showing only `.claude/launch.json`), plus: the user's PATH value (HKCU `Environment`) hashed unchanged; the firewall rules naming `citar` or `python` listed unchanged (P5.18.1 rule 11); no `%LOCALAPPDATA%\CITAR\app` or `\bin`; no `{9C1D2A54-...}_is1` uninstall key; no Start menu `CITAR` folder; in WSL no `~/.local/bin/citar*` or `~/.local/share/citar-app` for `claude`, no `citar` user, `/opt/citar*`, `/var/lib/citar*`, `/etc/citar`, citar unit or staging slice, nginx (once a package has installed it) stopped and disabled, no running container; on GitHub the environments, tags and releases unchanged.
- **S11** `python scripts/release.py check` (from 5-01).
- **S12** where signing is touched: no private key in the tree, the ignored files or the package's history (Phase 4's key scan), `update-keys.pub` 0 bytes.
- **CI-only** gates as named per package; nobody dispatches anything outside P5.18.2.

CI timing lessons stand: speed budgets times three under `CI`, durations by `perf_counter`, `TZ=UTC0`, `fsutil.newest_first`, waits on the condition with a deadline (never sleep-then-assert), Windows runners elevated as `runneradmin`, macOS limits soft.

### P5.18.6 Process

As Phase 4: a worktree per package (`.claude/worktrees/p-5-xx`, branch `p-5-xx`, from the previous package's merge); `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` before every cargo or maturin command; files written with the Write tool (Git Bash mangles backslashes in heredocs); small commits ending with the Co-Authored-By line; never `ops/`, `saves/` or `.claude/launch.json`. Each package ends with its gates, a review and a fix round, its as-built note with its start and end times, and handoff notes in `ops/p5-notes.md` (gitignored), apt installs in WSL included.

---

## P5.19 Decisions taken in this design

1. **One release build, `package.yml`,** run on every pull request and called by release.yml, which builds nothing itself.
2. **Cargo's version is the source;** `release.py` checks and bumps the rest; a dry run bumps its runner's checkout, so it builds exactly what a tag would.
3. **The label** is `v<version>` on a tag, `v<version>-dryrun+<sha>` on a dry run, `ci+<sha>` otherwise, on every release build.
4. **Wheels are built natively** on five runners with maturin 1.15.0: abi3-py311, manylinux_2_28, macOS 11; each smoke-tested on its runner on 3.11 and 3.14, and all five must report one build and play one seeded lab job to one result hash.
5. **The sdist is built into a wheel as a user would** and smoke-tested.
6. **The release's tested dependency set is an asset** (`server-constraints`), used by the image and the VPS.
7. **The helper ships twice:** unversioned self-updating binaries (the download links, the manifest, `--worker`) and versioned `packaged` archives (Homebrew, Scoop, winget).
8. **A dry run proves the signing path with a throwaway key** and two verifiers; the real key signs only a tag, under the environment's reviewer, and must match a committed public key; the signing job builds and installs nothing (a prebuilt xtask, the runner's Python), and its environment is the tag expression, so no dry run can name it.
9. **Every publishing job is tag-push-only** (`event_name == 'push'` and a `refs/tags/v` ref), proven by `check_workflows.py`; every action in every workflow is pinned by SHA; the called workflows get no secrets; package.yml's artifacts are `pkg-` and downloaded by name.
10. **Attestations only for a tag,** the files' made before anything is published and the image's after its push; the dry run shows their subjects.
11. **The image pushed is the image tested** (saved, loaded, pushed by architecture, joined); both architectures built natively on `python:3.12-slim-trixie`; `:latest` and `:<major.minor>` move only after the GitHub release is published.
12. **The GitHub release is a draft until whole,** then published, so `latest` never lacks a helper.
13. **The install scripts install wheels only** and say so on an unsupported platform (a free-threaded Python included); `--worker` installs the self-updating helper with its checksum; the helper's Linux builds share the game's glibc 2.28 floor.
14. **The Homebrew tap ships only `citar-helper`;** the game's formula is deleted.
15. **Third-party notices** are generated by xtask and travel with every binary.
16. **The server runs from a release layout** (`/opt/citar/releases/<v>`, `current`), units in the state directory, one unit definition carrying the lab's old CPU and IO weights, no `citar-lab`, no `requirements.txt`.
17. **`citar-deploy` scripts install, cutover, rollback and uncutover,** each with `--plan`; the laptop fetches and verifies the wheel; the snapshot is taken with the service stopped just before the switch, and a failed release that migrated is rolled back with that snapshot; the box's other sites are checked before and after, and only CITAR's own failure rolls back automatically.
18. **The staging run** reproduces the VPS's layout and limits in WSL (the CPU scaled to the VPS's, the other sites' memory and CPU held, swappiness 10, the job host sized for one CPU and 961 MB, every limit probed), including the cutover from 0.1.5, and Phase 6 repeats it before the real deploy.
19. **The bundle's job host and the MCP command** are fixed for installed copies; `doctor` proves the job host everywhere.
20. **The Linux suite runs 3.14 too.**

## P5.20 Risks

1. **CI-only platforms fail late.** macOS, arm64, Inno, Scoop, winget and Homebrew are first seen at a merge push. Mitigation: package.yml on PR #5 at every merge push, watched; each package's local stand-ins; the three-round bound with a report.
2. **The runners change** (Inno Setup absent from an image, winget missing, a macOS image retired). Mitigation: each tool found or installed in its job, its version printed; a missing optional check (winget install) reported, never silently skipped.
3. **A release run partly publishes.** Mitigation: the signature and both verifiers, then the files' attestations, all before anything irreversible; PyPI's `skip-existing` for a re-run; the release a draft until whole; the moving image tags last; RELEASING.md says what to re-run at each point.
4. **The VPS's memory and CPU.** The server and its lab now share one cgroup. Mitigation: measured in staging under limits proven to bind, with the other sites' memory and CPU held and the job host sized as on the box; `MemoryHigh` sized to the server's peak plus the host's figure; `CPUWeight=`/`IOWeight=` from the response-time gate; `OOMScoreAdjust` keeps the other sites safe.
5. **Dependency drift between the test and the deploy.** Mitigation: the constraints asset.
6. **The environment rule.** Mitigation: by construction, not by observation: every environment but `pypi` is the tag expression, empty on a dispatch (rule 14); every dispatch still reads the environments before and after, and a change stops the phase.
7. **Actions may refuse an empty environment name.** Then the first dry run (5-09) fails at startup, naming and creating nothing. Mitigation: P5.11's fallback, a dispatch-only `release-dry-run.yml`, made in 5-09's fix rounds.
8. **Staging's CPU scaling is an estimate** (about 60% of a laptop core for the VPS's vCPU). Mitigation: the figure is an argument and recorded; Phase 6 compares a lab game's speed on the box with staging's and records the ratio before any later rehearsal.
9. **A tag-only job runs for the first time on the real tag.** Mitigation: rules 2 and 11 to 16, the dry run's summary of what each would do, the order that puts every irreversible step after signing and attestation, and RELEASING.md's re-run table.
10. **The WSL disk** grows with images and targets. Mitigation: one target, prunes, the exit's `podman system reset` and removals, measured.
11. **winget** may never merge. Mitigation: nothing depends on it; the other routes cover Windows.

## P5.21 Questions for the owner, and what was settled without asking

**Two questions** (neither blocks a package: the design takes the default and makes the other answer one argument):
1. **The release number:** 0.1.6, as the branch and every design say, or 0.2.0 (plan §8's aside: "It might deserve the number 0.2.0")? **Answered (owner, 2026-10-19): 0.1.6.**
2. **The update-signing key before the 0.1.6 tag?** A helper trusts only the keys it was built with, so if 0.1.6 ships with `update-keys.pub` empty, no helper installed from 0.1.6 can ever update itself: each must be replaced by hand at 0.1.7. Default: the release proceeds either way (P5.4.2's first rule), and RELEASING.md's step 1 asks the owner to make `helper-signing` and commit the public key before tagging. **Answered (owner, 2026-10-19): yes.** The owner makes the key, `helper-signing` and its secret now; the public key line is committed to `update-keys.pub` in Phase 6 (RELEASING.md step 1), since Phase 5's gates (S12, 5-03 gate 3) hold that file empty.

**Settled without asking** (the plan, the owner's standing rules or common sense decide): the old Homebrew `citar` formula is deleted rather than deprecated (no other users; the CHANGELOG says how to move); Scoop and winget carry the helper as packaged builds; winget's manifests are still generated, validated and submitted in Phase 6 though Microsoft has merged none (minutes of work, no dependency on it); attestations only for a tag (a dry run would publish to a public log); the image moves to Debian 13 (Debian 12 is past regular security support); the Linux suite gains 3.14 (the classifiers claim it); release.yml does not call rust.yml or determinism.yml (Phase 6's bump pull request runs every workflow before the tag); the GitHub release is a draft until whole; the VPS's memory limits and CPU weights come from the staging measurement, not a guess; every action is pinned by SHA (Dependabot's `github-actions` updates are already configured, so no new automation is added); the Linux helpers are built in the manylinux_2_28 containers rather than given a second, higher floor; the image stays on Python 3.12, the VPS's (Dependabot's pull request #6 is superseded).

## P5.22 Work breakdown at a glance

Fifteen packages, strictly in this order, one agent at a time. Each ends with its gates, a review and a fix round, and its as-built note.

| # | Package | Plan §7 Phase 5 part |
|---|---|---|
| 5-01 | Versions, the build label and `release.py` | builds (release prerequisites) |
| 5-02a | The workflow guard, the pins and test.yml's call | builds |
| 5-02b | `package.yml`: the wheel matrix and the sdist | builds |
| 5-03 | The helper's release builds and the signing path | builds |
| 5-04 | Third-party notices | builds |
| 5-05 | The Windows bundle and installer (and the installed copies' fixes) | builds |
| 5-06 | The image from wheels, both architectures | builds |
| 5-07 | The install scripts: wheels only, the helper download | builds |
| 5-08 | Package managers and assembling a release | builds |
| 5-09 | release.yml end to end, the checklist, the first dry run | builds, exit |
| 5-10a | The server's release layout: units, backup, wrappers | VPS |
| 5-10b | `citar-deploy` and the deploy bundle | VPS |
| 5-11 | The staging dry run in WSL | VPS |
| 5-12 | The docs pass | docs |
| 5-13 | Phase 5 exit | exit |

The order: the version and label first, because every build uses them; the workflow guard and the pins before the first new workflow, so package.yml is born under its rules; the wheels before anything built from them (the bundle, the image, the install scripts, the deploy); the helper and notices before the package managers that ship them; `assemble` (5-08) before release.yml (5-09), which only publishes what package.yml assembled; the server's layout (5-10a) before the script that deploys into it (5-10b), and both after the release's asset names are fixed; the staging run when both exist; the docs pass when everything it describes exists; the exit last.

## P5.23 Review log (2026-10-18)

The design's review raised ten major and eleven minor findings. Each was checked against the tree, the plan and the private deploy log (read for facts only); all twenty-one hold, and each is fixed below. Where the fix differs from the one the review proposed, the reason is given.

| # | Finding (severity) | Checked against | Fix, and where | Package |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | A dry run creates `helper-signing` only if a skipped job never creates its environment: unverified platform behaviour, and detected only afterwards (major) | release.yml `:275`; P5.11 | Made true by construction: every environment but `pypi` is the tag expression, empty on a dispatch (P5.4.2, P5.11); rule 14 requires the form, with a planted literal failing; fallback if Actions refuses an empty name: a dispatch-only `release-dry-run.yml` (P5.11, risk 7). release.yml is reduced to its gate until 5-09, so its old signing job (a literal environment) is gone before rule 14 exists | 5-02a (rule, stub), 5-09 (jobs) |
| 2 | The signing key reaches every xtask build script and proc macro (major) | `.cargo/config.toml` (`xtask = "run ..."`); release.yml `:297-306` | The signing job builds and installs nothing: it runs the xtask binary package.yml's `helper-manifest` job built (`pkg-xtask-linux-x64`, with its hash) from `release_sign.py` on the runner's own Python, in the one step that holds the secret, checkouts with `persist-credentials: false` (P5.4.2). Not the review's other option of building xtask in an earlier step of the same job: a process an earlier step leaves running can read a later step's environment, and rule 8 forbids builds in release.yml. The `cryptography` verifier moved to a separate job without the secret (`check-signature`) instead of being installed in the signing job. Rule 15 holds all of it | 5-03 (artifact), 5-09 (job), 5-02a (rule) |
| 3 | Publishing and building jobs use moving action references; nothing forbids `secrets: inherit` on the calling jobs (major) | release.yml, every workflow (18 actions, about 120 references); `.github/dependabot.yml` | Rule 11 pins every remote `uses:` in **every** workflow by SHA (not only release.yml and package.yml: test.yml runs inside a release run and docs.yml holds `WIKI_TOKEN`); Dependabot's `github-actions` group already exists, so no new configuration is added; rule 12 for the calling jobs (P5.11, P5.17) | 5-02a |
| 4 | `attest` runs first on the real tag, after PyPI and `:latest` are public, and lacks `packages: write` (major) | P5.11's old table | `attest-files` before any publishing job; `attest-image` after the push with `packages: write` and its login; `:V-<arch>` and `:V` first, `:<major.minor>` and `:latest` in `image-tags` after the release; rule 16; RELEASING.md's re-run table (P5.11, P5.12, P5.15) | 5-09 |
| 5 | A release run shares one artifact namespace between test.yml and package.yml, and the full suite first runs bumped in 5-09's dry run; "nothing only release.yml catches" is false (major) | test.yml `:88-158` (`ext-<os>`, `helper-<os>`); release.yml `:283-286`, `:365` | `pkg-`/`rel-` prefixes and named downloads (P5.3.1, rule 13); 5-01 gate 5 runs the whole S1 suite and S7's nextest in the bumped clone; P5.18.4 rewritten to name what only a release run exercises and what covers each | 5-01, 5-02a, 5-02b |
| 6 | Automatic rollback after a migration crash-loops the old release; the backup is minutes stale; the bad-release gate never migrates; 0.1.5's backup script survives the deploy (major) | `citar/server/boot.py:55-110`; `deploy/citar-backup.service` | `install` records the revision, stops the service, snapshots, switches; a failed health check with a moved revision restores the snapshot before the previous release starts; `rollback` refuses an unknown revision without `--restore-backup`; `citar-backup` and `citar-secrets` installed with the units (P5.13.3); staging plants a migrating upgrade and a migrating failure (P5.14) | 5-10b, 5-11 |
| 7 | Staging measures another machine: the job host sized for WSL, pip and admin scopes outside the slice, no other sites, swappiness and CPU speed, limits never proven (major) | `citar/machine_settings.py:159-186`; WSL's cgroup files and `vm.swappiness` (read); the private log's measurement | All five taken: machine settings set to `host_cpu_defaults(mode="server", cpus=1, ram_mb=961)` with the host's ~240 MB asserted; `CITAR_DEPLOY_SLICE`/`--slice` for every `systemd-run`; a ballast (figure from the private log, kept in `ops/p5-notes.md`) and a CPU-loaded stand-in; swappiness 10, restored; `CPUQuota` 60% as a recorded argument (no way to measure the VPS's core from here; Phase 6 corrects it); probes that a memory, swap and CPU limit binds (P5.14) | 5-10a, 5-10b, 5-11 |
| 8 | The lab no longer yields CPU or IO to the other sites, and `MemoryHigh` throttles the server first (major) | `git show v0.1.5:deploy/citar-lab.service`; `deploy/citar.service` | `CPUWeight=`/`IOWeight=` on `citar.service` (20 to 50, settled by a stand-in response-time gate in staging); the sizing rule is now server peak plus host figure under `MemoryHigh`; the delegated child cgroup is the fallback, not the default, because `ProtectControlGroups=yes` makes `/sys/fs/cgroup` read-only to the unit and its combination with `Delegate=` on systemd 255 is unchecked (P5.13.2, P5.13.4, P5.14) | 5-10a, 5-11 |
| 9 | Linux helpers need glibc 2.35 while `install.sh` accepts 2.28 (major) | release.yml `:201-202`; helper.yml `:123-124`; `Cargo.toml:78-87` (rustls on ring) | Both Linux helpers built in the manylinux_2_28 containers, with an `objdump -T` check of the highest `GLIBC_` version, in package.yml and helper.yml; one floor for game and helper (P5.4.1, P5.8) | 5-03 |
| 10 | `citar.exe serve` in server mode binds `0.0.0.0` on the laptop and raises the firewall prompt (major) | `citar/settings.py:265` | P5.18.1 rule 11: every Windows server sets `CITAR_HOST=127.0.0.1` through `gate_run.py server`, which refuses otherwise; S10 lists the firewall rules before and after | 5-03 (harness), 5-05 |
| 11 | `assemble`'s one-label check includes the sdist build, which is `unknown` by design (minor) | P5.3.3, P5.10 | The label is checked across wheels, helpers, bundle and images; the sdist build for version, build id, jobs build and result hash, with its label asserted `unknown` (P5.10) | 5-08 |
| 12 | Gates reuse an earlier package's wheels that its merge deleted; several folders have no deletion step (minor) | P5.18.3, Appendix F | Each package builds its inputs from its own head (no shared wheel cache: 5-05's Python changes would make an earlier wheel wrong); every extra venv is `C:/dev/venv/p-5-xx-<purpose>`; staging logs under the package's target; 5-13 removes WSL's target, clones and podman storage, measured (P5.18.3) | every package, 5-13 |
